# Daily Summary: 2025-05-19

### The Hitchhikers Guide to Production-ready Trustworthy Foundation Model powered Software (FMware)
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.10640v1)
- **Authors**: Kirill Vasilevski, Benjamin Rombaut, Gopi Krishnan Rajbahadur, Gustavo A. Oliva, Keheliya Gallaba, Filipe R. Cogo, Jiahuei, Lin, Dayi Lin, Haoxiang Zhang, Bouyan Chen, Kishanthan Thangarajah, Ahmed E. Hassan, Zhen Ming, Jiang
- **Abstract**: Foundation Models (FMs) such as Large Language Models (LLMs) are reshaping the software industry by enabling FMware, systems that integrate these FMs as core components. In this KDD 2025 tutorial, we present a comprehensive exploration of FMware that combines a curated catalogue of challenges with real-world production concerns. We first discuss the state of research and practice in building FMware. We further examine the difficulties in selecting suitable models, aligning high-quality domain-specific data, engineering robust prompts, and orchestrating autonomous agents. We then address the complex journey from impressive demos to production-ready systems by outlining issues in system testing, optimization, deployment, and integration with legacy software. Drawing on our industrial experience and recent research in the area, we provide actionable insights and a technology roadmap for overcoming these challenges. Attendees will gain practical strategies to enable the creation of trustworthy FMware in the evolving technology landscape.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper, titled "The Hitchhiker's Guide to Production-ready Trustworthy Foundation Model powered Software (FMware)," outlines the challenges and a roadmap for developing and deploying software systems powered by Foundation Models (FMs) like Large Language Models (LLMs).  It introduces the term "FMware" to describe these systems and emphasizes the significant differences between traditional software engineering practices and those required for FMware. The paper details the FMware lifecycle, including FM selection and alignment, prompt engineering and grounding, agent orchestration, observability, and performance optimization.  It then critically examines the current state of practice, highlighting challenges in each stage, from model selection and alignment to compliance and maintenance. Finally, it proposes an action plan consisting of specific directives to address these challenges, aiming to bridge the gap between research and production-ready FMware.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's strength lies in its comprehensive and systematic overview of the challenges involved in building FMware. While many individual challenges have been discussed in isolation, this paper integrates them into a coherent framework, providing a holistic view of the FMware engineering process.  The term "FMware" itself provides a useful abstraction for discussing this class of systems. However, many of the challenges themselves are already recognized within the AI and software engineering communities. The paper's novelty is more in the compilation and structured analysis, rather than groundbreaking new discoveries.
*   **Significance:** The paper is highly significant because it addresses a critical need: the transition of FM/LLM-based systems from experimental prototypes to robust, trustworthy, and scalable production deployments.  The outlined challenges are real and substantial, and the action plan offers a practical roadmap for researchers and practitioners. By systematically identifying and categorizing the difficulties, the paper provides a valuable guide for navigating the complexities of FMware development. The clear and concise presentation of these complexities is a great starting point for both experts and newcomers, and provides the necessary vocabulary for further discussion in this area. However, the paper does not contain experimental results to back up its claims and only references existing research, limiting the impact of the proposed solutions.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   Comprehensive and systematic coverage of the FMware lifecycle.
    *   Clear articulation of the differences between traditional software and FMware engineering.
    *   Practical and actionable action plan with specific directives.
    *   Well-organized and easy-to-understand presentation.
    *   The 'FMware' abstraction is helpful for articulating this specific class of systems.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Lack of empirical validation of the proposed solutions.
    *   The action plan is primarily a set of directions for future research rather than concrete, tested solutions.
    *   Limited depth in addressing the ethical and societal implications of FMware.
    *   Relies heavily on existing literature and does not present novel technical contributions.

**Justification for the Score:**

The paper offers significant value by providing a comprehensive overview of the landscape and outlining a research agenda.  Its value as a curated catalogue of the challenges currently being faced in the field is undeniable. However, its limitations in empirical validation and depth, along with the incremental nature of its novelty, warrant a score slightly below the top tier. The absence of experiments or rigorous evaluation lowers its overall impact, despite the practical relevance of its findings.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 7/10

### Interpretable Risk Mitigation in LLM Agent Systems
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.10670v1)
- **Authors**: Jan Chojnacki
- **Abstract**: Autonomous agents powered by large language models (LLMs) enable novel use cases in domains where responsible action is increasingly important. Yet the inherent unpredictability of LLMs raises safety concerns about agent reliability. In this work, we explore agent behaviour in a toy, game-theoretic environment based on a variation of the Iterated Prisoner's Dilemma. We introduce a strategy-modification method-independent of both the game and the prompt-by steering the residual stream with interpretable features extracted from a sparse autoencoder latent space. Steering with the good-faith negotiation feature lowers the average defection probability by 28 percentage points. We also identify feasible steering ranges for several open-source LLM agents. Finally, we hypothesise that game-theoretic evaluation of LLM agents, combined with representation-steering alignment, can generalise to real-world applications on end-user devices and embodied platforms.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a method for mitigating risks and improving the interpretability of Large Language Model (LLM) agents in game-theoretic environments. The approach involves steering the LLM's residual stream using interpretable features extracted from a sparse autoencoder (SAE) latent space. The authors focus on the Iterated Prisoner's Dilemma (IPD) as a testing ground. They show that steering with features like "good-faith negotiation" can significantly reduce the agent's defection probability. They also identify feasible steering ranges for several open-source LLMs and propose that this combination of game-theoretic evaluation and representation-steering alignment could generalize to real-world applications.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Novelty of Approach:** The paper proposes a relatively novel approach by combining game-theoretic evaluation of LLM agents with representation steering based on sparse autoencoders. While SAEs and residual stream steering are not entirely new, their application in the context of IPD for AI safety and alignment is a valuable contribution.
    *   **Interpretability Focus:** A key strength is the emphasis on interpretability. By using SAE features, the authors attempt to ground the steering process in human-understandable concepts, making it easier to reason about and control the agent's behavior.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The paper provides empirical evidence demonstrating the effectiveness of their approach. The experiments in the IPD environment show that steering with specific features can influence the agent's strategy and reduce defection rates.
    *   **Open-Source Contribution:** The authors provide their code and data, promoting reproducibility and allowing other researchers to build upon their work.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scope of Environment:** The IPD, while a valuable theoretical model, is a simplified environment. The generalizability of the findings to complex real-world scenarios is questionable. The prompt is too simple and doesn't capture many edge cases, limiting the impact on other environments.
    *   **Interpretability Challenges:** Although SAE features are intended to be more interpretable, there is still a degree of abstraction and complexity involved. The connection between the SAE features and the agent's decision-making process may not always be perfectly clear. Also, the "good-faith negotiation" features aren't perfect, as they can be interpreted as bad-faith negotiation depending on the context.
    *   **Reliance on Sparse Autoencoders:** The reliance on pre-trained SAEs introduces potential biases and limitations. The quality and characteristics of the SAE features will directly influence the effectiveness of the steering process.
    *   **Lack of Broader Discussion of Ethical Considerations:** There are some ethical issues that arise from steering, as it could result in a dishonest output from the models.

*   **Significance:**

    *   The work is significant because it addresses the growing concerns about the safety and reliability of LLM agents. The proposed method provides a way to potentially align agent behavior with human values and mitigate risks.
    *   The focus on interpretability is also significant, as it can contribute to building more transparent and understandable AI systems.
    *   The paper provides a valuable foundation for further research in the area of safe and aligned LLM agents.

*   **Novelty:**

    *   The integration of game-theoretic evaluation with representation steering.
    *   The identification of interpretable features (e.g., "good-faith negotiation") that can influence agent behavior in a desirable way.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a valuable contribution to the field of AI safety and alignment. It proposes a novel method for mitigating risks in LLM agents, emphasizes interpretability, and provides empirical validation. However, the limitations of the IPD environment and the reliance on sparse autoencoders raise questions about the generalizability and robustness of the approach. Also, it uses already well-known techniques in sparse autoencoders and vector manipulations.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 6/10

### Embodied AI in Machine Learning -- is it Really Embodied?
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.10705v1)
- **Authors**: Matej Hoffmann, Shubhan Parag Patni
- **Abstract**: Embodied Artificial Intelligence (Embodied AI) is gaining momentum in the machine learning communities with the goal of leveraging current progress in AI (deep learning, transformers, large language and visual-language models) to empower robots. In this chapter we put this work in the context of "Good Old-Fashioned Artificial Intelligence" (GOFAI) (Haugeland, 1989) and the behavior-based or embodied alternatives (R. A. Brooks 1991; Pfeifer and Scheier 2001). We claim that the AI-powered robots are only weakly embodied and inherit some of the problems of GOFAI. Moreover, we review and critically discuss the possibility of cross-embodiment learning (Padalkar et al. 2024). We identify fundamental roadblocks and propose directions on how to make progress.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Embodied AI in Machine Learning – is it Really Embodied?":

**Summary:**

The paper critically examines the recent trend of applying large language models (LLMs) and vision-language models (VLMs) to robotics, often termed "Embodied AI" (WEAI).  It argues that this approach exhibits only weak embodiment, inheriting problems from Good Old-Fashioned AI (GOFAI), particularly the symbol grounding and frame problems. The authors argue that current WEAI focuses primarily on the "brain" (LLM/VLM), neglecting the critical role of body morphology, sensorimotor coordination, and active perception in truly embodied intelligence. The paper dissects how WEAI deviates from the principles of embodiment, highlighting issues with ecological balance, reliance on passive offline learning, and high computational costs. It questions the cross-embodiment learning paradigm, suggesting that the abstraction layer created by standardizing robot actions undermines the essence of embodied intelligence. The paper concludes by exploring potential future directions, including active learning within a single embodiment, incorporating tactile sensing effectively, and revisiting the design of foundation models specifically for robotics.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper provides a valuable and timely critique of the current hype surrounding "Embodied AI." Its strength lies in its well-structured analysis, clearly articulating the differences between true embodiment and the superficial integration of LLMs/VLMs into robotics. By framing the discussion within the historical context of GOFAI and behavior-based robotics, the authors offer a compelling argument that the field risks repeating past mistakes. The embodiment checklist provides a useful tool for evaluating the "embodiedness" of current systems.

Here are the points to consider:
*   **Novelty:** The paper's critique isn't entirely novel. Many researchers within robotics have voiced similar concerns about the over-reliance on large models and the neglect of fundamental principles of embodied intelligence. However, the paper synthesizes these concerns into a cohesive and accessible framework. It offers a concise summary of the current state of the field. The authors have drawn a clear comparison with GOFAI and embodied AI and provided a helpful checklist.
*   **Significance:** The paper's significance resides in its potential to re-orient the research direction within the embodied AI field. The paper emphasizes the problem and suggests the solution. By clearly articulating the limitations of current approaches and highlighting the importance of active learning, sensorimotor coordination, and body morphology, it could inspire researchers to focus on more grounded and ecologically valid approaches. The paper could trigger many research directions in a novel setting.
*   **Depth of Analysis:** While the critique is well-articulated, the paper could benefit from a deeper exploration of potential solutions. The suggestions for future directions are somewhat broad and lack concrete implementation details. However, this is understandable given the scope of a chapter.
*   **Clarity and Accessibility:** The paper is well-written and relatively accessible, even to readers not deeply familiar with the history of AI and robotics.
*   **Influence:** The paper has a potential to be highly influential, specifically within the broader robotics community which is grappling with the LLM trend.
*   **Weakness:** The paper focuses mostly on identifying the problems but does not include solutions to solve them.

**Justification of Score:**

While the paper's critique isn't entirely novel, the clear and well-structured presentation of the argument, along with the checklist, makes it a significant contribution to the ongoing debate within the field. The paper serves as a valuable reality check, prompting researchers to reconsider the meaning of "embodiment" in the context of AI-powered robots. It effectively contrasts the shallow integration of LLMs/VLMs with the richer, more ecologically valid approaches that emphasize the critical role of the body and its interaction with the environment. Given its potential to redirect research efforts and foster more grounded approaches, I assign it a score of 7.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### SafeTrans: LLM-assisted Transpilation from C to Rust
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.10708v1)
- **Authors**: Muhammad Farrukh, Smeet Shah, Baris Coskun, Michalis Polychronakis
- **Abstract**: Rust is a strong contender for a memory-safe alternative to C as a "systems" programming language, but porting the vast amount of existing C code to Rust is a daunting task. In this paper, we evaluate the potential of large language models (LLMs) to automate the transpilation of C code to idiomatic Rust, while ensuring that the generated code mitigates any memory-related vulnerabilities present in the original code. To that end, we present the design and implementation of SafeTrans, a framework that uses LLMs to i) transpile C code into Rust and ii) iteratively fix any compilation and runtime errors in the resulting code. A key novelty of our approach is the introduction of a few-shot guided repair technique for translation errors, which provides contextual information and example code snippets for specific error types, guiding the LLM toward the correct solution. Another novel aspect of our work is the evaluation of the security implications of the transpilation process, i.e., whether potential vulnerabilities in the original C code have been properly addressed in the translated Rust code. We experimentally evaluated SafeTrans with six leading LLMs and a set of 2,653 C programs accompanied by comprehensive unit tests, which were used for validating the correctness of the translated code. Our results show that our iterative repair strategy improves the rate of successful translations from 54% to 80% for the best-performing LLM (GPT-4o), and that all types of identified vulnerabilities in the original C code are effectively mitigated in the translated Rust code.
- **Summary**: The paper "SafeTrans: LLM-assisted Transpilation from C to Rust" investigates the use of Large Language Models (LLMs) for automating the translation of C code to memory-safe Rust code, with a focus on mitigating memory-related vulnerabilities. The authors introduce SafeTrans, a framework that uses LLMs to transpile C code into Rust and iteratively repair compilation and runtime errors. A key contribution is a few-shot guided repair technique for translation errors, which provides contextual information and example code snippets to guide the LLM towards correct solutions. The paper also evaluates the security implications of transpilation, assessing whether potential vulnerabilities in the original C code are addressed in the translated Rust. Experiments are conducted with six LLMs and a dataset of 2,653 C programs with unit tests. The results show that the iterative repair strategy improves translation success rates and all identified vulnerabilities in the original C code are effectively mitigated.

**Critical Evaluation of Novelty and Significance:**

The paper presents a well-executed study with several novel contributions, but also suffers from limitations that temper its overall significance.

*   **Novelty:** The key novelty lies in the guided repair technique, which enhances the LLM's ability to correct translation errors by providing targeted instructions and examples. This goes beyond simply feeding compiler errors back to the LLM and demonstrates a more structured approach to leveraging LLMs for code repair. The evaluation of security implications is also a valuable contribution, as many previous studies have mainly focused on functional correctness without scrutinizing the resulting code for improved security.
*   **Significance:** The problem of migrating legacy C code to memory-safe languages like Rust is undeniably important. This paper offers a practical approach for automating this process, potentially reducing the effort and cost involved. Demonstrating the effectiveness of LLMs in this domain, particularly in mitigating vulnerabilities, is a significant step. The benchmark itself (2653 C programs) is valuable to the community, although relying on test cases from CodeNet which has questionable code is problematic.

However, the study has some notable limitations:

*   **Data Set Dependence:** The findings are highly dependent on the characteristics of the CodeNet dataset. While the authors perform static analysis and ESBMC, the types of programs in CodeNet (competitive programming solutions) may not be representative of real-world C codebases. The paper notes that the dataset largely uses `scanf()`, arrays, and simple arithmetic operations. This leads to a bias towards identifying certain types of vulnerabilities. More complex memory management patterns or concurrency issues found in systems code may not be adequately captured.
*   **ESBMC Limitations:** The paper acknowledges that ESBMC is bounded and therefore cannot guarantee the absence of all bugs. The study therefore only provides a best-effort identification of potential vulnerabilities. While it is valuable, demonstrating that the translations prevent *exploits* against known vulnerabilities would have significantly strengthened the impact and increased the paper's credibility.
*   **LLM Selection:** While the LLM selection is diverse, the landscape of LLMs is rapidly changing. There has been significant advancement since the models used in this paper, and the results may not fully reflect the capabilities of the most cutting-edge LLMs currently available.
*   **Evaluation Scope:** The evaluation primarily focuses on compilation and runtime correctness as defined by the original C unit tests. While this is a necessary step, it doesn't fully guarantee the *quality* of the translated code or its robustness in unforeseen scenarios. Human review and further testing would be needed to ensure the translated code is truly production-ready. There is limited human evaluation, or metrics that measure idiomatic Rust code being produced.

Despite these limitations, the paper represents a significant contribution to the field of automated code translation and vulnerability mitigation using LLMs. The guided repair technique and security-focused evaluation are valuable additions. The primary limitation lies in the dataset's nature, which limits the generalizability of the findings to all types of C code.

Score: 7

**Rationale:** A score of 7 reflects the paper's novelty in the guided repair approach and security evaluation, and its significance in automating C-to-Rust translation. However, the dependence on the CodeNet dataset and ESBMC and lack of exploration of the LLM's generated idiomatic rust significantly limits the generalizability and impact. Further research with more realistic C codebases, deeper exploration of Rust-specific safety guarantees, human evaluation and more stringent validation techniques are needed to fully realize the potential of LLMs in this domain.

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 7/10

### A Modular Approach for Clinical SLMs Driven by Synthetic Data with Pre-Instruction Tuning, Model Merging, and Clinical-Tasks Alignment
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.10717v1)
- **Authors**: Jean-Philippe Corbeil, Amin Dada, Jean-Michel Attendu, Asma Ben Abacha, Alessandro Sordoni, Lucas Caccia, François Beaulieu, Thomas Lin, Jens Kleesiek, Paul Vozila
- **Abstract**: High computation costs and latency of large language models such as GPT-4 have limited their deployment in clinical settings. Small language models (SLMs) offer a cost-effective alternative, but their limited capacity requires biomedical domain adaptation, which remains challenging. An additional bottleneck is the unavailability and high sensitivity of clinical data. To address these challenges, we propose a novel framework for adapting SLMs into high-performing clinical models. We introduce the MediPhi collection of 3.8B-parameter SLMs developed with our novel framework: pre-instruction tuning of experts on relevant medical and clinical corpora (PMC, Medical Guideline, MedWiki, etc.), model merging, and clinical-tasks alignment. To cover most clinical tasks, we extended the CLUE benchmark to CLUE+, doubling its size. Our expert models deliver relative improvements on this benchmark over the base model without any task-specific fine-tuning: 64.3% on medical entities, 49.5% on radiology reports, and 44% on ICD-10 coding (outperforming GPT-4-0125 by 14%). We unify the expert models into MediPhi via model merging, preserving gains across benchmarks. Furthermore, we built the MediFlow collection, a synthetic dataset of 2.5 million high-quality instructions on 14 medical NLP tasks, 98 fine-grained document types, and JSON format support. Alignment of MediPhi using supervised fine-tuning and direct preference optimization achieves further gains of 18.9% on average.
- **Summary**: Here's a concise summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel framework for adapting Small Language Models (SLMs) into high-performing clinical models. The core components of the framework are: (1) Pre-Instruction Tuning (PIT) on diverse medical and clinical corpora to create domain-specific experts, (2) Model Merging to unify these experts and preserve gains across various benchmarks, and (3) Clinical-Tasks Alignment using a newly created synthetic dataset, MediFlow, which consists of 2.5 million high-quality instructions on various medical NLP tasks. The authors also extend the CLUE benchmark to CLUE+ to cover a wider range of clinical tasks.  The resulting model, MediPhi, demonstrates significant improvements over base models and in some cases surpasses GPT-4 in specific tasks like ICD-10 coding.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Novelty:** The paper introduces a modular approach combining pre-instruction tuning, model merging, and clinical task alignment which is a significant contribution.
    *   **MediFlow Dataset:** The generation and release of the MediFlow synthetic dataset addresses a critical limitation in the field: the scarcity and sensitive nature of clinical data.  The size and diversity of this dataset are commendable.
    *   **Extensive Benchmarking:** The comprehensive evaluation using the extended CLUE+ benchmark provides a thorough analysis of the model's performance across various tasks.
    *   **Performance Gains:** The reported improvements over base models, and in some cases even GPT-4, demonstrates the effectiveness of the proposed framework.
    *   **Open-source artifact:** The release of the models and dataset under a permissive license will likely boost adoption and further research.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Synthetic Data Limitations:**  While MediFlow is a significant asset, it is ultimately synthetic data. The potential biases and limitations introduced by the GPT-4-based generation process need to be further explored. The paper could have benefited from analysis about distribution difference between real clinical data and the MediFlow.
    *   **Generalization concerns:** While CLUE+ is more diverse than prior benchmarks, it still might not fully capture the complexity of real-world clinical scenarios. Future work should focus on evaluating the model in more realistic settings with uncurated real clinical data.
    *   **Ablation studies, hyperparameter tuning, architectural details** It is not clear how much time was dedicated to hyperparameter tuning, and the description of architectures is limited.
    *   **Limited exploration of merging techniques:** Other merging strategies (beyond Task Arithmetic, TIES, BreadCrumbs) could have been explored.

*   **Significance:**

    *   The paper has a high potential impact in enabling wider adoption of SLMs in clinical settings, offering a cost-effective alternative to LLMs.
    *   The MediFlow dataset is a valuable resource for the community and will likely foster further research in clinical NLP.
    *   The framework provides a practical guide for adapting SLMs to specific domains, addressing the challenges of data scarcity and domain adaptation.

*   **Justification of Score:**

    The paper's strengths, particularly the development of MediFlow and the demonstrated performance gains, make it a valuable contribution to the field. However, the synthetic nature of the data and the need for further validation in real-world scenarios temper its immediate impact. The paper offers a solid, well-executed approach to clinical NLP, pushing the boundaries of SLM application and dataset creation.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### AI-enhanced semantic feature norms for 786 concepts
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.10718v1)
- **Authors**: Siddharth Suresh, Kushin Mukherjee, Tyler Giallanza, Xizheng Yu, Mia Patil, Jonathan D. Cohen, Timothy T. Rogers
- **Abstract**: Semantic feature norms have been foundational in the study of human conceptual knowledge, yet traditional methods face trade-offs between concept/feature coverage and verifiability of quality due to the labor-intensive nature of norming studies. Here, we introduce a novel approach that augments a dataset of human-generated feature norms with responses from large language models (LLMs) while verifying the quality of norms against reliable human judgments. We find that our AI-enhanced feature norm dataset, NOVA: Norms Optimized Via AI, shows much higher feature density and overlap among concepts while outperforming a comparable human-only norm dataset and word-embedding models in predicting people's semantic similarity judgments. Taken together, we demonstrate that human conceptual knowledge is richer than captured in previous norm datasets and show that, with proper validation, LLMs can serve as powerful tools for cognitive science research.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel AI-enhanced method (NOVA) for generating semantic feature norms for 786 concepts. It combines human-sourced feature lists with large language model (LLM) imputation and verification to create a richer and more comprehensive norm dataset. The authors demonstrate that the resulting AI-enhanced norm dataset exhibits higher feature density, greater overlap among concepts, and improved performance in predicting human semantic similarity judgments compared to human-only norms and word embeddings. The study highlights the potential of leveraging LLMs, with careful validation, to augment and improve semantic norm datasets for cognitive science research.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Novel Approach:** The paper presents a genuinely innovative method for generating semantic feature norms by strategically combining human input with LLM capabilities. This hybrid approach addresses the limitations of both purely human-generated (labor-intensive, limited coverage) and purely LLM-generated (hallucinations, non-human-like knowledge) norms.
*   **Large-Scale Dataset:** The resulting dataset (NOVA) is significant in size, encompassing a substantial number of concepts and features, enhancing its potential for use in various research areas.
*   **Rigorous Validation:** The authors conducted thorough validation experiments, comparing different LLM models and prompting strategies to ensure the quality and reliability of the AI-enhanced norms.
*   **Improved Performance:** The empirical results demonstrate that NOVA significantly outperforms both human-only norms and word embeddings in predicting human semantic similarity judgments, providing strong evidence for its effectiveness.
*   **Reproducibility and Accessibility:** The authors emphasize open-source tools and models, and the intention to release code and the dataset fosters reproducibility and accessibility for the research community.
*   **Addresses Existing Limitations:** The paper effectively tackles the trade-offs faced by traditional norming studies regarding concept/feature coverage and quality control.
*   **Addresses Concerns of LLM Hallucinations:** By utilizing LLMs for imputation followed by verification with other LLMs they directly attempt to address concerns of inaccuracies.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Reliance on LLM-based Verification:** Although validated, the verification step relies on another LLM (GPT-40). While the choice of GPT-40 is justified, any biases or limitations inherent in that model may still influence the final dataset. There is a potential concern of LLM bias being introduced, although the authors do state that human judgement was used to compare the outputs of different LLMs.
*   **Subjectivity in Semantic Judgments:** Semantic similarity judgments are inherently subjective. While the authors use majority-vote to mitigate this, there may still be variability and individual differences not fully captured by the norms.
*   **Limited Scope of Validation:** The validation experiments primarily focus on semantic similarity judgments. While important, it would be beneficial to assess NOVA's performance on a broader range of cognitive tasks, such as priming, categorization, or property verification.
*   **Dependence on Specific LLMs:** The study's results are contingent on the specific LLMs used (Flan-T5 and GPT-40). Future research should explore the generalizability of the approach with different LLMs and architectures.
*   **Limited error analysis:** It would be useful to have a more thorough error analysis demonstrating where the enhanced data improves the information obtained in the original data.

**Novelty and Significance:**

The paper presents a significant advance in the field of semantic feature norms. It addresses limitations of existing methods and opens up new possibilities for studying human conceptual knowledge. The NOVA dataset and the AI-enhanced methodology hold promise for:

*   **Cognitive Science:** Providing a more comprehensive and accurate resource for modeling human semantic memory and cognitive processes.
*   **Computational Linguistics:** Improving the development of semantic representations and language understanding systems.
*   **Artificial Intelligence:** Enabling the creation of more human-like and robust AI systems that can reason about concepts and relationships.
*   **Replicable Workflow:** Providing a workflow for the research community to construct similar datasets using this hybrid human and model approach, advancing future research.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper is a strong contribution, but not without limitations. The combination of novelty, rigor, and potential impact warrants a high score. However, a perfect score is not justified given the reliance on LLMs for verification and the limited scope of validation experiments.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### Tracr-Injection: Distilling Algorithms into Pre-trained Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.10719v1)
- **Authors**: Tomás Vergara-Browne, Álvaro Soto
- **Abstract**: Motivated by the surge of large language models, there has been a push to formally characterize the symbolic abilities intrinsic to the transformer architecture. A programming language, called RASP, has been proposed, which can be directly compiled into transformer weights to implement these algorithms. However, the tasks that can be implemented in RASP are often uncommon to learn from natural unsupervised data, showing a mismatch between theoretical capabilities of the transformer architecture, and the practical learnability of these capabilities from unsupervised data. We propose tracr-injection, a method that allows us to distill algorithms written in RASP directly into a pre-trained language model. We showcase our method by injecting 3 different algorithms into a language model. We show how our method creates an interpretable subspace within the model's residual stream, which can be decoded into the variables present in the code of the RASP algorithm. Additionally, we found that the proposed method can improve out of distribution performance compared to our baseline, indicating that indeed a more symbolic mechanism is taking place in the inner workings of the model. We release the code used to run our experiments.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Tracr-Injection: Distilling Algorithms into Pre-trained Language Models":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces *tracr-injection*, a method for distilling algorithms, written in the RASP (Regular Architecture for Semantic Processing) programming language, directly into the parameters of pre-trained language models (LLMs). RASP allows direct compilation of code into transformer weights, enabling the implementation of specific algorithms. The authors aim to bridge the gap between the theoretical capabilities of transformers (as demonstrated by RASP) and their practical ability to learn these capabilities from unsupervised data. Tracr-injection involves fine-tuning an LLM to linearly encode the residual stream of a RASP-compiled transformer model. The approach creates an interpretable subspace within the LLM's residual stream that corresponds to variables in the original RASP code. Experiments showcase the injection of three different algorithms (Shuffle-Dyck language recognition, character counting, and two-number addition) into a GPT-2 model. The method's benefits include improved out-of-distribution performance compared to a baseline, suggesting the incorporation of more symbolic mechanisms, and the ability to decode and interpret the model's inner workings.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of injecting RASP-compiled algorithms into LLMs to imbue them with symbolic capabilities is genuinely novel. The combination of RASP, tracr, and distillation techniques for this purpose is a unique contribution. The demonstration of creating interpretable subspaces within LLMs and causally linking them to algorithmic variables is also a key strength. Prior works have explored either offloading token generation or causal abstractions, but injecting ground truth algorithms directly into the LLM's weights is a differentiating factor. The method offers a fresh approach to combining the strengths of pre-trained language models and symbolic AI, rather than simply leveraging one for the other (e.g. LLM as a high-level planner for low-level symbolic execution).

*   **Significance:** The significance of the paper stems from several potential impacts:

    *   **Interpretability:** The ability to decode the residual stream and link it to known algorithmic variables is a crucial step toward understanding and controlling LLM behavior. This opens the door to more rigorous interpretability studies and the validation of automated circuit discovery techniques. The authors correctly identify that lacking ground truth for evaluation is a key issue, and this helps resolve it.
    *   **Symbolic Reasoning:** By injecting symbolic structures, the approach potentially enhances LLMs' reasoning capabilities and their ability to generalize to unseen scenarios. This can have practical benefits for tasks requiring logical inference and constraint satisfaction.
    *   **Robustness:** Injection of algorithms can potentially make the model more robust, in the case that we understand what it is being robust to.
    *   **Bridging Symbolic and Connectionist AI:**  RASP is able to provide a theoretically grounded way of combining deep learning with symbolic programs.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The method is well-motivated by the limitations of current LLMs in symbolic reasoning and the potential for integrating symbolic AI.
    *   The tracr-injection process is clearly explained, with sufficient technical detail.
    *   The experiments provide evidence of improved out-of-distribution performance and the creation of interpretable subspaces.
    *   The analysis provides compelling arguments that the injected representations are causally relevant to the model's predictions.
    *   Acknowledges the need to benchmark interpretability methods based on injected ground truths, a key contribution.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The experimental results are based on a relatively small GPT-2 model.  Scaling the method to larger, more powerful models is necessary to demonstrate its practical utility.
    *   The three algorithms used in the experiments are relatively simple.  Exploring more complex and realistic tasks is important.
    *   The limitation on out-of-distribution performance generalization suggests that more work is needed to transfer learned algorithms to other LLMs effectively.
    *   The reliance on RASP as the symbolic code is not fully justified. What if we use a better DSL than RASP? How can the architecture of the compiled model be leveraged and improved?

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence research in several areas:

    *   Mechanistic Interpretability:  By enabling the study of LLMs with known ground-truth algorithms, the paper can stimulate the development of new and improved interpretability techniques.
    *   Symbolic AI Integration: Tracr-injection can inspire new approaches to combining symbolic and connectionist AI, leading to more powerful and robust AI systems.
    *   Neuro-Symbolic AI: Further development of this technique can produce models more likely to be considered "neuro-symbolic".

**Justification for Score:**

Considering the paper's novelty, significance, strengths, and weaknesses, I assign a score of **7**. While the work presents a novel approach with significant potential for advancing interpretability and symbolic reasoning in LLMs, the limitations concerning model scale, task complexity, and out-of-distribution generalization need to be addressed in future research to fully realize its impact. The successful scaling of the technique can lead to greater levels of abstraction for neurosymbolic programming of neural networks, which is why the method is high impact.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Automating Security Audit Using Large Language Model based Agent: An Exploration Experiment
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.10732v1)
- **Authors**: Jia Hui Chin, Pu Zhang, Yu Xin Cheong, Jonathan Pan
- **Abstract**: In the current rapidly changing digital environment, businesses are under constant stress to ensure that their systems are secured. Security audits help to maintain a strong security posture by ensuring that policies are in place, controls are implemented, gaps are identified for cybersecurity risks mitigation. However, audits are usually manual, requiring much time and costs. This paper looks at the possibility of developing a framework to leverage Large Language Models (LLMs) as an autonomous agent to execute part of the security audit, namely with the field audit. password policy compliance for Windows operating system. Through the conduct of an exploration experiment of using GPT-4 with Langchain, the agent executed the audit tasks by accurately flagging password policy violations and appeared to be more efficient than traditional manual audits. Despite its potential limitations in operational consistency in complex and dynamic environment, the framework suggests possibilities to extend further to real-time threat monitoring and compliance checks.
- **Summary**: Okay, let's break down this paper:

**Summary:**

The paper explores the feasibility of using Large Language Models (LLMs) as autonomous agents to automate security audits, specifically focusing on password policy compliance in Windows operating systems. The authors use GPT-4 with the Langchain framework to design an agent that can audit users and machines against predefined password policies. The experiments involve prompting the agent with various scenarios and evaluating its ability to identify password policy violations. The results suggest that LLMs can automate certain aspects of security audits, reducing time and improving consistency, though limitations exist in handling ambiguous data and complex environments. The paper concludes that LLMs hold promise for enhancing cybersecurity but require further refinement and human oversight.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Practical Application:** The paper addresses a relevant and practical problem: the time-consuming and costly nature of manual security audits. Automating these tasks using LLMs has the potential to improve efficiency and reduce human error.
*   **Clear Methodology:** The methodology is well-defined, outlining the setup, agent architecture (single-agent and ReAct), the LLM used (GPT-4), the agentic tools (Shell Command Interpreter and CIS Password Policy PDF Reader), and the experiment design. This makes the paper reproducible and easy to understand.
*   **Well-defined experiments:** The experimental setup with different scenarios and evaluation metrics (Interpret Task, Independent Task Execution, Evaluate Task Result) ensures a systematic evaluation.
*   **Discussion of Limitations:** The paper acknowledges limitations such as the agent's inability to handle ambiguous data, its reliance on accurate prompts, potential biases in the model, and the need for human oversight. This indicates a realistic perspective on the capabilities and challenges of using LLMs in security audits.
*   **Clearly articulates future directions** such as refining prompts, enhancing model transparency and privacy preserving techniques.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Novelty:**  The core idea of using LLMs for automation is not entirely new. While the specific application to password policy audits is practical, it doesn't represent a significant theoretical breakthrough. LLMs are already being researched in threat detection and vulnerability identification. The novelty primarily lies in its specific instantiation and validation within the realm of password policy compliance.
*   **Simplified Environment:** The experiments are conducted in a controlled environment. A real-world environment is more dynamic and complex, with variations in system configurations, user behavior, and security policies. The paper's results may not directly translate to real-world performance.
*   **Limited Comparative Analysis:** The paper focuses primarily on demonstrating the feasibility of the LLM-based agent. There is no comparison against other automation tools or techniques. It is unclear to what extent the LLM agent outperforms existing solutions. Without this benchmark, it is difficult to assess its relative effectiveness.
*   **Specific scope** The experimental results are based on a very specific scenario (password policy on windows machines). It remains unclear how it could adapt or scale to other broader compliance categories.

**Significance:**

The paper contributes to the growing body of research on using LLMs for cybersecurity tasks. It demonstrates that LLMs can automate specific components of security audits and can significantly reduce time for field security auditors. The experiments shed light on the capabilities and limitations of LLMs in this domain.

**Justification for Score:**

While the paper is well-written and addresses a relevant problem, its novelty is somewhat limited.  There's a clear execution of a practical application, but the conceptual contribution to the field of LLMs in security is not transformative. The limitations mentioned also temper the excitement of the results. The paper does, however, provide a concrete example of how LLMs can be applied to security tasks, which can inspire further research and development in this area.

Therefore, I assign a score of:

**Score: 6**

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 6/10

### IMAGE-ALCHEMY: Advancing subject fidelity in personalised text-to-image generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.10743v1)
- **Authors**: Amritanshu Tiwari, Cherish Puniani, Kaustubh Sharma, Ojasva Nema
- **Abstract**: Recent advances in text-to-image diffusion models, particularly Stable Diffusion, have enabled the generation of highly detailed and semantically rich images. However, personalizing these models to represent novel subjects based on a few reference images remains challenging. This often leads to catastrophic forgetting, overfitting, or large computational overhead.We propose a two-stage pipeline that addresses these limitations by leveraging LoRA-based fine-tuning on the attention weights within the U-Net of the Stable Diffusion XL (SDXL) model. First, we use the unmodified SDXL to generate a generic scene by replacing the subject with its class label. Then, we selectively insert the personalized subject through a segmentation-driven image-to-image (Img2Img) pipeline that uses the trained LoRA weights.This framework isolates the subject encoding from the overall composition, thus preserving SDXL's broader generative capabilities while integrating the new subject in a high-fidelity manner. Our method achieves a DINO similarity score of 0.789 on SDXL, outperforming existing personalized text-to-image approaches.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Image-Alchemy: Advancing Subject Fidelity in Personalized Text-to-Image Generation":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a two-stage pipeline ("Image-Alchemy") to improve subject fidelity in personalized text-to-image generation using Stable Diffusion XL (SDXL).  The first stage involves fine-tuning the attention weights of SDXL's U-Net using LoRA (Low-Rank Adaptation) with a small number of reference images of the target subject.  The second stage leverages the unmodified SDXL to generate a generic scene with the subject's class label.  Finally, it uses a segmentation-driven Img2Img pipeline, guided by the LoRA weights, to insert the personalized subject into the generic scene. This approach aims to preserve SDXL's broader generative capabilities while achieving high-fidelity subject representation.  The authors report a DINO similarity score of 0.789, claiming superior performance over existing methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Addresses a Relevant Problem:**  Personalized text-to-image generation is a challenging and important area, particularly regarding fidelity, catastrophic forgetting, and computational cost.
*   **Two-Stage Approach is a Good Idea:** Decoupling subject encoding from scene generation is conceptually strong and potentially helpful in preserving SDXL's existing knowledge.  Segmentation-based insertion is a logical extension.
*   **Use of LoRA:** Employing LoRA for efficient fine-tuning is a practical choice to reduce computational overhead and memory requirements.
*   **Clear Presentation:** The paper's methodology is relatively well-explained.
*   **The framework preserves SDXL's capabilities:** By utilizing LoRA, it can introduce new subjects in a fast, lightweight manner.
*   **It mitigates key challenges:** This includes token selection, catastrophic forgetting, and excessive overfitting.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Novelty:** The individual components (LoRA fine-tuning, segmentation-based Img2Img) are well-established. The novelty lies in the specific combination and application to the SDXL framework, but this incremental nature somewhat limits the paper's impact.
*   **Evaluation Concerns:**  The comparison against existing methods is somewhat vague. While the DINO score is presented, the baselines and specific configurations aren't fully detailed, hindering a rigorous comparative assessment. More detailed comparisons with more standard metrics across a greater range of diverse subjects would enhance the evaluation. Lack of a user study to validate subject fidelity.
*   **Lack of Detail on Token Selection:** The token selection strategy is described, but more examples and justification would be helpful. The effectiveness is contingent on finding truly "rare" tokens, and the paper doesn't thoroughly demonstrate this.
*   **Experimentation is Limited:** Using only 4-5 reference images is a very low number, which could limit the quality of the LoRA model and how well it adapts to different prompts.
*   **Overfitting Claim Contradictory:**  The paper mentions intentional overfitting. But the problem with this approach could be not only a loss of generalization but also a degradation of the image.
*   **No ablation studies:** It lacks detail on the contribution of LoRA and the segmentation-driven Img2Img process.

**Significance:**

While the approach is sensible, the overall significance is somewhat limited by the incremental nature of the method and evaluation quality. Although Image-Alchemy provides a simple and efficient approach to personalized image generation, its approach seems to be lacking key aspects of experimentation which will hinder its impact.

**Justification:**

The paper presents a logical pipeline that builds upon established techniques in text-to-image generation.  The strength of the paper lies in the practicality of combining LoRA and segmentation, enabling efficient subject adaptation and preventing the model from forgetting the details of the original subject. The primary weaknesses are its fairly incremental nature and limited experimental evaluation. The reported DINO score needs stronger validation. This diminishes the paper's overall significance.

**Score: 5**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 5/10

### Code-Driven Planning in Grid Worlds with Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.10749v1)
- **Authors**: Ashwath Vaithinathan Aravindan, Zhisheng Tang, Mayank Kejriwal
- **Abstract**: We propose an iterative programmatic planning (IPP) framework for solving grid-based tasks by synthesizing interpretable agent policies expressed in code using large language models (LLMs). Instead of relying on traditional search or reinforcement learning, our approach uses code generation as policy synthesis, where the LLM outputs executable programs that map environment states to action sequences. Our proposed architecture incorporates several prompting strategies, including direct code generation, pseudocode-conditioned refinement, and curriculum-based prompting, but also includes an iterative refinement mechanism that updates code based on task performance feedback. We evaluate our approach using six leading LLMs and two challenging grid-based benchmarks (GRASP and MiniGrid). Our IPP framework demonstrates improvements over direct code generation ranging from 10\% to as much as 10x across five of the six models and establishes a new state-of-the-art result for GRASP. IPP is found to significantly outperform direct elicitation of a solution from GPT-o3-mini (by 63\% on MiniGrid to 116\% on GRASP), demonstrating the viability of the overall approach. Computational costs of all code generation approaches are similar. While code generation has a higher initial prompting cost compared to direct solution elicitation (\$0.08 per task vs. \$0.002 per instance for GPT-o3-mini), the code can be reused for any number of instances, making the amortized cost significantly lower (by 400x on GPT-o3-mini across the complete GRASP benchmark).
- **Summary**: Here is a concise summary and critical evaluation of the paper, including a novelty/significance score and justification:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Iterative Programmatic Planning (IPP), a novel framework that utilizes Large Language Models (LLMs) to solve grid-based planning tasks by generating interpretable code as agent policies. IPP employs an iterative refinement loop, where LLM-generated programs are executed, evaluated, and revised based on performance feedback. The framework incorporates different prompting strategies, including direct code generation, pseudocode-conditioned refinement, and curriculum-based prompting. The authors evaluate IPP on GRASP and MiniGrid benchmarks using several leading LLMs, demonstrating significant performance improvements compared to direct action sequence elicitation and other baselines.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its unique approach to planning: viewing planning as *code generation* instead of direct action sequence generation. This is a significant shift from traditional search or reinforcement learning methods. The incorporation of an iterative refinement process, guided by task-specific feedback, further enhances the novelty, allowing the LLM to learn and correct errors in its generated code. While code generation for other tasks is not new, applying it specifically and iteratively to *planning* in gridworlds, coupled with performance-based refinement, is a novel contribution. The structured prompting strategies, such as pseudocode extension and curriculum-based prompting, also add to the paper's originality.

**Significance:** The paper's significance stems from several factors:

*   **Improved Performance:** IPP demonstrates substantial performance gains over direct prompting methods and other baselines on challenging benchmarks, showcasing the viability of code-driven planning.
*   **Interpretability:** The generated code-based policies are inherently more interpretable than opaque policies produced by RL or end-to-end neural models. This interpretability is crucial for debugging, verification, and potentially adaptation by humans.
*   **Efficiency:** The amortized cost of code-based policies is much lower than that of direct action generation, as the generated code can be reused across multiple task instances. This makes IPP a potentially scalable and practical solution.
*   **Generalization:** The code-driven approach enables the creation of reusable policies that generalize across different task instances, avoiding the per-instance inference required by other LLM-based methods.
*   **New State-of-the-Art:** IPP sets a new state-of-the-art result for the GRASP benchmark.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Problem Formulation:** The paper clearly defines the planning problem and motivates the code-driven approach.
*   **Novel Framework:** The IPP framework is well-designed and effectively integrates code generation, iterative refinement, and structured prompting strategies.
*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The experiments are conducted on multiple benchmarks and with several leading LLMs, providing robust evidence for the effectiveness of IPP.
*   **Detailed Analysis:** The paper provides a thorough analysis of the results, highlighting the strengths and weaknesses of different prompting strategies and LLMs.
*   **Well-Written and Organized:** The paper is clearly written, well-organized, and easy to follow.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Dependence on LLM Understanding:** The framework's performance is inherently limited by the LLM's understanding of the environment and task rules, which can lead to errors and inconsistencies.
*   **Cost and Time Involved:** The iterative refinement process can be computationally expensive and time-consuming, requiring multiple model invocations and evaluations.
*   **Feedback Precision:** The feedback signal used for refinement is relatively coarse-grained, potentially hindering the model's ability to correct fine-grained errors.
*   **Limited Environments:** The focus on grid-based environments, while suitable for initial validation, limits the generalizability of the approach to more complex and continuous environments.
*   **Limited Safeguard Discussion**: The negative impacts are mentioned but not deeply analyzed; nor are potential safeguards well explored.

**Potential Influence:**

The paper's approach of treating planning as code generation has the potential to significantly influence the field, particularly in the context of integrating LLMs with robotics and embodied AI. The interpretability and efficiency of code-based policies make them attractive for safety-critical and resource-constrained applications. Future research can build upon this work by exploring more sophisticated refinement mechanisms, extending the framework to continuous environments, and incorporating more diverse forms of feedback.

**Score: 7**

**Justification:** The paper presents a novel and well-executed approach to planning using LLMs. It offers significant performance improvements, enhances interpretability, and provides a potentially more efficient and scalable alternative to existing methods. The comprehensive evaluation and detailed analysis strengthen the paper's contribution. The weaknesses related to LLM dependence, computational cost, and environmental limitations are acknowledged, but they do not detract significantly from the overall significance of the work. The paper has a clear path for future research and the potential to influence the field of planning with LLMs, thus meriting a score of 7.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### Unifying Segment Anything in Microscopy with Multimodal Large Language Model
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.10769v1)
- **Authors**: Manyu Li, Ruian He, Zixian Zhang, Weimin Tan, Bo Yan
- **Abstract**: Accurate segmentation of regions of interest in biomedical images holds substantial value in image analysis. Although several foundation models for biomedical segmentation have currently achieved excellent performance on certain datasets, they typically demonstrate sub-optimal performance on unseen domain data. We owe the deficiency to lack of vision-language knowledge before segmentation. Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) bring outstanding understanding and reasoning capabilities to multimodal tasks, which inspires us to leverage MLLMs to inject Vision-Language Knowledge (VLK), thereby enabling vision models to demonstrate superior generalization capabilities on cross-domain datasets. In this paper, we propose using MLLMs to guide SAM in learning microscopy crose-domain data, unifying Segment Anything in Microscopy, named uLLSAM. Specifically, we propose the Vision-Language Semantic Alignment (VLSA) module, which injects VLK into Segment Anything Model (SAM). We find that after SAM receives global VLK prompts, its performance improves significantly, but there are deficiencies in boundary contour perception. Therefore, we further propose Semantic Boundary Regularization (SBR) to prompt SAM. Our method achieves performance improvements of 7.71% in Dice and 12.10% in SA across 9 in-domain microscopy datasets, achieving state-of-the-art performance. Our method also demonstrates improvements of 6.79% in Dice and 10.08% in SA across 10 out-ofdomain datasets, exhibiting strong generalization capabilities. Code is available at https://github.com/ieellee/uLLSAM.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Unifying Segment Anything in Microscopy with Multimodal Large Language Model":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces uLLSAM, a novel framework that leverages Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) to improve the performance and generalization capabilities of Segment Anything Model (SAM) in microscopy image segmentation. uLLSAM aims to overcome the limitations of existing microscopy foundation models like µSAM, which often struggle with cross-domain data due to a lack of vision-language knowledge. The core of uLLSAM consists of two modules: a Vision-Language Semantic Alignment (VLSA) module, which injects vision-language knowledge into SAM, and a Semantic Boundary Regularization (SBR) module, which enhances SAM's boundary awareness. The authors demonstrate that uLLSAM achieves state-of-the-art performance on both in-domain and out-of-domain microscopy datasets, showcasing its strong generalization abilities. The paper also presents a user-friendly graphical interface for ease of use.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in the integration of MLLMs with SAM for microscopy image segmentation. While MLLMs have been applied to other computer vision tasks, their application to microscopy, specifically to guide SAM's learning and improve cross-domain generalization, appears to be a relatively new direction. The VLSA and SBR modules, designed specifically to address the challenges of microscopy images, also contribute to the novelty.
    *   **Key Contributions:**  Introducing MLLMs into microscopy image analysis to facilitate domain transfer is the largest contribution. The SBR boundary regularization is a clever, domain-specific adaptation.
    *   **Potential Weaknesses:** The individual components (SAM, MLLMs) are not novel. The novelty resides in the *specific combination* and adaptation of these components for microscopy. It's unclear if the VLSA module is fundamentally different from other vision-language alignment techniques.

*   **Significance:**  Accurate segmentation is crucial for biomedical image analysis. The improved performance and generalization demonstrated by uLLSAM could significantly benefit researchers by enabling more efficient and accurate analysis of microscopic imagery across diverse imaging conditions and modalities. The potential to reduce reliance on domain-specific models and expert annotation is also significant.
    *   **Strengths:** The results on various datasets (both in-domain and out-of-domain) are compelling and show a consistent improvement over existing methods like µSAM. The paper discusses both the technical approach and the user-friendly interface, increasing its potential impact.
    *   **Weaknesses:** The paper lacks a thorough analysis of the limitations of the VLSA and SBR modules. It would be helpful to understand scenarios where these modules might fail or underperform. Furthermore, the paper acknowledges limitations related to text utilization and hallucinations, and the quality of textual description outputs. The discussion around limitations is candid, but highlights that there is still considerable room for improvement. There isn't a deep dive into the computational resources required for training and inference, limiting its practical assessment. The evaluation metric, Segmentation Accuracy (SA), could be considered less robust than Intersection over Union (IoU). The benefits need to be weighed against the increased complexity of using MLLMs, including computational cost and training data quality.

*   **Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel and potentially significant contribution to the field of biomedical image analysis. The application of MLLMs to guide SAM in microscopy segmentation is innovative, and the experimental results demonstrate a clear improvement in performance and generalization. However, there are still notable limitations that need to be addressed, particularly regarding the VLSA module's unique contribution, hallucination control, computational resources, and potential failure cases. Further exploration of these limitations, and comparison to other transfer learning techniques in similar data regimes, would solidify the paper's impact. While the paper demonstrates the potential of the approach, its long-term impact will depend on future research that addresses these limitations.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Ranked Voting based Self-Consistency of Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.10772v1)
- **Authors**: Weiqin Wang, Yile Wang, Hui Huang
- **Abstract**: Majority voting is considered an effective method to enhance chain-of-thought reasoning, as it selects the answer with the highest "self-consistency" among different reasoning paths (Wang et al., 2023). However, previous chain-of-thought reasoning methods typically generate only a single answer in each trial, thereby ignoring the possibility of other potential answers. As a result, these alternative answers are often overlooked in subsequent voting processes. In this work, we propose to generate ranked answers in each reasoning process and conduct ranked voting among multiple ranked answers from different responses, thereby making the overall self-consistency more reliable. Specifically, we use three ranked voting methods: Instant-runoff voting, Borda count voting, and mean reciprocal rank voting. We validate our methods on six datasets, including three multiple-choice and three open-ended question-answering tasks, using both advanced open-source and closed-source large language models. Extensive experimental results indicate that our proposed method outperforms the baselines, showcasing the potential of leveraging the information of ranked answers and using ranked voting to improve reasoning performance. The code is available at https://github.com/szu-tera/RankedVotingSC.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Ranked Voting based Self-Consistency of Large Language Models":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel approach to improve the self-consistency of large language models (LLMs) by incorporating ranked voting. Instead of generating a single answer for each chain-of-thought reasoning path, the proposed method generates ranked answers based on likelihood. These ranked answers are then aggregated using ranked voting methods like instant-runoff voting, Borda count voting, and mean reciprocal rank voting to determine the final answer. The authors demonstrate through experiments on various datasets that this approach consistently outperforms traditional majority voting-based self-consistency baselines, showing the potential of leveraging the information encoded in ranked answers to improve reasoning performance.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The core idea of integrating ranked voting with self-consistency is reasonably novel. Traditional self-consistency methods typically rely on single answers generated across multiple reasoning paths, potentially discarding valuable information about alternative possibilities. By generating and leveraging ranked answers, the paper offers a way to consider the model's uncertainty and preference ordering, which represents an improvement in the methodology.

**Significance:** The significance of this work lies in its potential to improve the reliability and accuracy of LLMs in reasoning tasks. Self-consistency has already been shown to be an effective technique, and the ranked voting enhancement further boosts performance across several datasets and models. The improvements, while not always dramatic, are consistent, which suggests that this approach could be a valuable addition to the LLM toolkit. The experiments cover a good range of open-source and closed-source models which further adds to the value of the paper. The robustness analysis showing improvement with shuffled data is also a very valuable addition.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Problem Statement:** The paper clearly identifies a limitation in traditional self-consistency methods, namely the omission of valuable information contained in alternative answers.
*   **Well-Defined Approach:** The ranked voting methodology is well-defined and uses established voting mechanisms, making the approach accessible and understandable.
*   **Extensive Experiments:** The authors conduct experiments on a diverse set of datasets and models, providing strong empirical evidence for the effectiveness of their approach.
*   **Robustness Analysis:** The method demonstrates greater robustness across example orderings than baseline approaches.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Incremental Improvement:** While the improvements are consistent, they are often incremental rather than revolutionary. In other words, while the method works, it doesn’t provide a massive jump in performance.
*   **Computational Overhead:** Generating ranked answers inherently increases the computational cost compared to generating single answers. While the paper doesn't directly address this trade-off, it is a relevant consideration for practical applications.
*   **Limited Voting Strategies:** While the selected voting methods are well-established, the paper could benefit from exploring other potentially more suitable ranked voting mechanisms for LLM outputs (e.g., Condorcet methods), or provide a rigorous comparison of their chosen methods.
*   **Tie-Breaker Rule Dependency**: The tie breaking mechanism might affect the efficiency of the model. A more rigorous evaluation of the impact of this dependency could be beneficial.

**Justification:**

The paper is a good contribution to the field because it addresses a real limitation of self-consistency methods and offers a reasonable solution. The experiments are comprehensive, and the results are encouraging. However, the limited improvement and the lack of a thorough analysis of the different voting strategies and computational costs prevent this work from being exceptional. The results show slight improvements that could still be useful in improving chain-of-thought based prompting in various applications. Overall, the strengths outweigh the weaknesses.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Context-Aware Probabilistic Modeling with LLM for Multimodal Time Series Forecasting
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.10774v1)
- **Authors**: Yueyang Yao, Jiajun Li, Xingyuan Dai, MengMeng Zhang, Xiaoyan Gong, Fei-Yue Wang, Yisheng Lv
- **Abstract**: Time series forecasting is important for applications spanning energy markets, climate analysis, and traffic management. However, existing methods struggle to effectively integrate exogenous texts and align them with the probabilistic nature of large language models (LLMs). Current approaches either employ shallow text-time series fusion via basic prompts or rely on deterministic numerical decoding that conflict with LLMs' token-generation paradigm, which limits contextual awareness and distribution modeling. To address these limitations, we propose CAPTime, a context-aware probabilistic multimodal time series forecasting method that leverages text-informed abstraction and autoregressive LLM decoding. Our method first encodes temporal patterns using a pretrained time series encoder, then aligns them with textual contexts via learnable interactions to produce joint multimodal representations. By combining a mixture of distribution experts with frozen LLMs, we enable context-aware probabilistic forecasting while preserving LLMs' inherent distribution modeling capabilities. Experiments on diverse time series forecasting tasks demonstrate the superior accuracy and generalization of CAPTime, particularly in multimodal scenarios. Additional analysis highlights its robustness in data-scarce scenarios through hybrid probabilistic decoding.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces CAPTime, a novel framework for multimodal time series forecasting that leverages large language models (LLMs).  CAPTime addresses limitations in existing LLM-based forecasting methods, particularly their shallow integration of exogenous textual data and their reliance on deterministic decoding. The key innovations include: (1) a text abstraction module that captures semantically relevant information from text for each time step, enabling fine-grained multimodal fusion; (2) a mixture-of-experts (MoE) approach combined with frozen LLM parameters for context-aware probabilistic decoding, preserving LLM's distribution modeling capabilities while allowing for adaptive forecasting based on textual context. Extensive experiments across diverse time series datasets demonstrate CAPTime's superior accuracy and generalization, especially in multimodal settings, and its robustness in data-scarce scenarios.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper presents several noteworthy innovations. The text abstraction module for fine-grained temporal alignment of text and time series data is a significant advancement over existing prompting or shallow fusion techniques. Integrating MoE with a frozen LLM is a clever approach to maintaining the LLM's pre-trained knowledge while enabling context-aware distribution modeling. While other works have explored LLMs for time series, CAPTime provides a more sophisticated architecture for multimodal integration and probabilistic forecasting.

* **Significance:** Time series forecasting is a crucial task, and the ability to effectively integrate multimodal data, particularly textual information, has substantial practical value. The paper convincingly demonstrates CAPTime's improved performance over state-of-the-art methods, particularly in scenarios where multimodal data is important. The results on few-shot and zero-shot forecasting also highlight the potential of CAPTime for adapting to new domains and datasets with limited training data, which is a significant advantage in real-world applications.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Comprehensive Experiments:** The paper includes extensive experiments across various datasets and forecasting horizons, providing strong empirical evidence for CAPTime's effectiveness.
    * **Detailed Ablation Studies:** The ablation studies thoroughly analyze the contribution of each component of the CAPTime framework, justifying the design choices.
    * **Clear and Well-Written:** The paper is well-structured and clearly explains the proposed method and the experimental results.
    * **Addresses a Key Limitation:** The paper directly tackles a key limitation in LLM-based time series forecasting: the effective fusion of textual context.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Complexity:** The CAPTime framework introduces additional complexity compared to simpler LLM-based approaches. While the authors justify this complexity with improved performance, the increased computational cost and engineering effort might be a barrier to adoption in some cases.
    * **LLM Selection:** The paper primarily uses GPT-2 as the base LLM. While this allows for comparison with previous work, exploring the performance with more recent and powerful LLMs (e.g., Llama2, Qwen) would further strengthen the results.  The paper acknowledges that pre-trained LLMs contribute to context-alignment, so it seems plausible that future LLMs would be even more beneficial.
    * **Text Abstraction is "black box":** While the text abstraction module is effective, the paper lacks an explanation of _which_ textual elements are prioritized. In other words, what aspects of the context drive downstream predictions?

* **Potential Impact:** CAPTime has the potential to significantly advance the field of time series forecasting, especially in applications where multimodal data is available. Its ability to effectively integrate textual context and perform probabilistic forecasting could lead to more accurate and robust predictions in various domains, including finance, healthcare, and traffic management. The focus on few-shot and zero-shot learning is also important for adapting to new domains and datasets with limited resources.

* **Justification for Score:**  CAPTime represents a significant advancement over existing LLM-based time series forecasting methods, particularly in the area of multimodal integration and probabilistic modeling. The rigorous experimental evaluation and detailed ablation studies provide strong evidence for its effectiveness.  While the complexity of the framework and the need to use a dated LLM version are shortcomings, the overall contribution is substantial. The potential impact on various application domains warrants a high score.
