### Evolutionary training-free guidance in diffusion model for 3D multi-objective molecular generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.11037v1)
- **Authors**: Ruiqing Sun, Dawei Feng, Sen Yang, Yijie Wang, Huaimin Wang
- **Abstract**: Discovering novel 3D molecular structures that simultaneously satisfy multiple property targets remains a central challenge in materials and drug design. Although recent diffusion-based models can generate 3D conformations, they require expensive retraining for each new property or property-combination and lack flexibility in enforcing structural constraints. We introduce EGD (Evolutionary Guidance in Diffusion), a training-free framework that embeds evolutionary operators directly into the diffusion sampling process. By performing crossover on noise-perturbed samples and then denoising them with a pretrained Unconditional diffusion model, EGD seamlessly blends structural fragments and steers generation toward user-specified objectives without any additional model updates. On both single- and multi-target 3D conditional generation tasks-and on multi-objective optimization of quantum properties EGD outperforms state-of-the-art conditional diffusion methods in accuracy and runs up to five times faster per generation. In the single-objective optimization of protein ligands, EGD enables customized ligand generation. Moreover, EGD can embed arbitrary 3D fragments into the generated molecules while optimizing multiple conflicting properties in one unified process. This combination of efficiency, flexibility, and controllable structure makes EGD a powerful tool for rapid, guided exploration of chemical space.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces EGD (Evolutionary Guidance in Diffusion), a novel training-free framework for generating 3D molecular structures using diffusion models and evolutionary algorithms.  EGD iteratively refines molecular candidates by applying evolutionary operators (crossover, mutation) to noise-perturbed samples and then denoising the results using a pre-trained diffusion model. This approach allows for conditional generation based on multiple properties and the integration of user-defined structural fragments without retraining the diffusion model. The authors demonstrate EGD's effectiveness on single and multi-objective molecular generation tasks, showing superior performance compared to existing conditional diffusion methods in terms of accuracy, speed, and flexibility. EGD allows for the design of protein ligands with specific structural constraints by enabling the incorporation of desired 3D fragments and optimization of properties simultaneously.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in seamlessly integrating evolutionary operators into the diffusion sampling process in a *training-free* manner. Existing methods typically require retraining for new properties or rely on classifier gradients. Embedding crossover and mutation within the denoising loop provides a novel approach to navigating the chemical space, blending structural fragments from different molecules in a controlled way. The ability to incorporate structural constraints *without* requiring modifications to the diffusion model or the need for any gradient information is significant. That said, evolutionary algorithms and diffusion models, separately, are well-established, so the novelty is in the combination and the demonstrated effectiveness of that combination.

*   **Significance:** The potential impact on the field of molecular design is considerable. The speed advantage (up to five times faster), coupled with the flexibility of specifying structural constraints and optimizing for multiple properties concurrently, addresses critical limitations of existing approaches.  The ability to generate customized protein ligands with specific structures directly benefits drug discovery efforts by allowing researchers to rapidly explore and refine lead candidates. EGD's training-free nature makes it significantly more accessible and adaptable than methods requiring extensive retraining for different objectives. The approach offers a way to bypass the limitations imposed by training-specific objectives, which is crucial in the dynamic chemical discovery process. Furthermore, the idea of combining generative deep learning models with evolutionary algorithms could inspire new research directions in other scientific domains beyond molecular design.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The training-free aspect is a significant advantage, increasing accessibility and flexibility.
    *   The method demonstrates superior performance on both single and multi-objective tasks.
    *   The ability to embed arbitrary 3D fragments is a practical benefit for molecular design.
    *   Experimental results are comprehensive, comparing against multiple state-of-the-art baselines and across different datasets.
    *   The method addresses the issue of chemical validity when combining evolutionary algorithms, which is crucial for practical applications.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   While the method is training-free at generation time, the performance relies on a pre-trained diffusion model. The quality of this model significantly affects the results.
    *   The selection of appropriate fitness functions is still crucial and problem-dependent, requiring domain expertise.
    *   The method's efficacy might degrade when the user-defined constraints are very stringent, or the target objectives fall outside the diffusion model's training data distribution.
    *   The paper could benefit from a more detailed analysis of the sensitivity to the choice of evolutionary parameters (population size, mutation rate, etc.)

*   **Potential Influence:**  EGD has the potential to become a widely adopted tool for molecular generation due to its efficiency, flexibility, and ease of use. The paper's contribution lies in addressing the current bottlenecks within the domain that include task-specific training requirements, rigid conditioning mechanisms, and the absence of a unified framework for balancing diverse objectives. Its impact could range from accelerating drug discovery to facilitating the design of novel materials with tailored properties. The core concept of embedding evolutionary operators within diffusion sampling could lead to similar frameworks being developed in other generative modeling applications.

**Score: 8**

**Justification:** The paper presents a novel and effective approach to molecular generation that overcomes significant limitations of existing methods. The integration of evolutionary algorithms with diffusion models in a training-free framework is innovative, and the experimental results demonstrate its superior performance and flexibility. While the method relies on a pre-trained diffusion model and requires careful design of fitness functions, the benefits outweigh these limitations. The potential impact on the field of molecular design is substantial, making this a significant contribution. The approach represents a valuable advancement, but would require additional insights, such as specific details about its integration in current chemical workflows, to be truly transformative.

- **Classification**: cs.NE
- **Score**: 8/10

### Efficient Attention via Pre-Scoring: Prioritizing Informative Keys in Transformers
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.11040v1)
- **Authors**: Zhexiang Li, Haoyu Wang, Yutong Bao, David Woodruff
- **Abstract**: Recent advances in transformer architectures deeply enhance long-context language modeling. Among them, HyperAttention achieves competitive efficiency by combining a single-level LSH-based clustering with uniform residual sampling. However,such a sampling limits crucial keys' capturing, which in turn raises the overall perplexity. In this paper, we propose a pre-scoring mechanism to assist HyperAttention to prioritize significant keys. Specifically, we introduce three scoring methods: K-means clustering, K-median clustering, and leverage score-based ranking (inspired by LevAttention) to filter keys effectively. We further replace HyperAttention's original uniform residual sampling entirely, relying exclusively on our pre-scoring mechanism. Experiments on ChatGLM2 (131k token context) reduce perplexity from 12 to 8.3, which outperforms standard HyperAttention. Moreover, when running on the Vision-Transformer (ViT), our method shows that it can guarantee similar accuracy compared with LevAttention, and will surpass LevAttention given specific parameters. Although this method introduces computational overhead, its combination with HyperAttention remains 20 times faster than FlashAttention, providing a balanced trade-off between speed and modeling accuracy. Our results highlight the effectiveness of integrating pre-scoring into hierarchical attention mechanisms, significantly improving Transformer's efficiency.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a pre-scoring mechanism to improve the efficiency and accuracy of transformer attention mechanisms, particularly HyperAttention. It addresses HyperAttention's limitation in identifying all significant keys, which leads to suboptimal perplexity.  The proposed method uses clustering (k-means, kernel k-means, k-median) to prioritize informative keys before applying HyperAttention. This pre-scoring step filters out less important keys, allowing HyperAttention to focus on the most relevant information.  The method also replaces HyperAttention's uniform residual sampling with the pre-scoring mechanism entirely. The paper presents experimental results on language models (ChatGLM2) and vision transformers (ViT), showing improved perplexity and accuracy compared to standard HyperAttention and, in some cases, LevAttention.  The paper also includes theoretical analysis, demonstrating that the clustering-based pre-scoring can recover heavy keys under certain planted-subspace models, performing at least as well as leverage score-based methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The core idea of pre-scoring keys using clustering before applying an efficient attention mechanism like HyperAttention has merit. The combination aims to balance speed and accuracy, which is a crucial concern in large language models. Replacing uniform residual sampling with clustering and achieving performance gains demonstrates a clear improvement over existing methods. The planted-subspace model and its analysis, showing the effectiveness of k-means pre-scoring, add a theoretical underpinning that isn't always present in applied transformer papers. The exploration of different clustering techniques (k-means, k-median, kernel k-means) and the empirical comparison are also valuable. It's important to note that pre-scoring techniques, and the use of k-means for data selection have been explored previously (cited in the paper: [1]), which somewhat reduces the overall novelty, but the specific combination with HyperAttention and theoretical analysis are novel.

* **Significance:** Improving the efficiency and accuracy of attention mechanisms is a very significant area of research, especially as models continue to grow in size and context length. If the proposed method consistently outperforms HyperAttention without sacrificing speed gains relative to FlashAttention, it represents a valuable contribution. The experimental results, particularly the perplexity reduction on ChatGLM2, are promising. The ViT results, although more nuanced, suggest potential benefits in vision tasks as well. A key aspect of significance is whether this pre-scoring technique can be generalized to other efficient attention variants beyond HyperAttention.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies the limitations of HyperAttention and proposes a targeted solution.
    * **Comprehensive Experiments:** The paper provides results on both language and vision models, comparing against relevant baselines.
    * **Theoretical Justification:**  The inclusion of the planted-subspace model strengthens the theoretical basis of the method.
    * **Practical Relevance:** The focus on improving efficiency makes the work highly relevant to real-world applications.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Limited Generalization Analysis:** While the paper shows improvements, the analysis is primarily empirical. It would be beneficial to explore the factors that determine when this pre-scoring approach is most effective.
    * **Dependency on HyperAttention:** The method relies heavily on HyperAttention.  It would be valuable to assess whether the same pre-scoring can enhance other attention approximations (e.g., Linformer). The experiments primarily showcase the improvement with HyperAttention, reducing some of the broader impact.
    * **Overhead of Pre-Scoring:** The overhead introduced by the pre-scoring mechanism should be carefully considered. While the paper claims it is near-linear and maintains speed advantages, more detailed runtime analyses, including profiling, would be helpful, especially when varying context lengths and dimensionality.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper's approach could influence researchers working on efficient transformer architectures. The idea of using clustering or similar techniques to prioritize keys could be adopted in other contexts. The theoretical analysis provides a useful framework for evaluating similar methods. The results encourage further exploration of pre-scoring mechanisms in hierarchical attention architectures.

* **Justification of Score:** The paper presents a solid contribution with a clear idea, supporting experiments, and some theoretical backing. While the novelty is somewhat incremental (building upon existing pre-scoring concepts and HyperAttention), the performance gains and the theoretical analysis provide a valuable contribution to the field. The dependence on a specific efficient attention mechanism (HyperAttention) and limited generalizability analysis reduces the potential for wider adoption. Thus, a good but not outstanding score is justified.

### HSRMamba: Efficient Wavelet Stripe State Space Model for Hyperspectral Image Super-Resolution
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.11062v1)
- **Authors**: Baisong Li, Xingwang Wang, Haixiao Xu
- **Abstract**: Single hyperspectral image super-resolution (SHSR) aims to restore high-resolution images from low-resolution hyperspectral images. Recently, the Visual Mamba model has achieved an impressive balance between performance and computational efficiency. However, due to its 1D scanning paradigm, the model may suffer from potential artifacts during image generation. To address this issue, we propose HSRMamba. While maintaining the computational efficiency of Visual Mamba, we introduce a strip-based scanning scheme to effectively reduce artifacts from global unidirectional scanning. Additionally, HSRMamba uses wavelet decomposition to alleviate modal conflicts between high-frequency spatial features and low-frequency spectral features, further improving super-resolution performance. Extensive experiments show that HSRMamba not only excels in reducing computational load and model size but also outperforms existing methods, achieving state-of-the-art results.
- **Summary**: Okay, I've reviewed the provided paper "HSRMamba: Efficient Wavelet Stripe State Space Model for Hyperspectral Image Super-Resolution." Here's a summary and a critical evaluation.

**Summary:**

The paper introduces HSRMamba, a novel single hyperspectral image super-resolution (SHSR) model based on the Visual Mamba architecture.  HSRMamba addresses the limitations of existing Visual Mamba models, which use a 1D scanning scheme that can introduce artifacts. HSRMamba incorporates a stripe-based scanning scheme to better capture global and local features, reducing artifacts.  It also employs wavelet decomposition to separate and model low-frequency spectral features and high-frequency spatial features, mitigating modal conflicts. The model consists of three main modules: Low-Frequency Spectral Encoder (LFSE), High-Frequency Spatial Encoder (HFSE), and High-Frequency and Low-Frequency Fusion Decoder (HLFD).  Experimental results on three hyperspectral datasets demonstrate that HSRMamba outperforms existing SHSR methods in terms of both performance and computational efficiency.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper offers several novel contributions, but their individual impact varies.

    *   **Stripe-Based Scanning:** The introduction of a stripe-based scanning scheme is a worthwhile adaptation of the visual Mamba, but the *idea* of using overlapping stripes in image processing is not inherently novel. The specific *application* to Visual Mamba *and* its proven effectiveness *in the context of HSI super-resolution* is the key contribution here. This addresses the limitations of existing 1D scanning approaches.

    *   **Wavelet Decomposition for Modal Conflict:**  The use of wavelet decomposition to separate spatial and spectral features *before* processing within a Mamba-based architecture is more original. Spectral and spatial information often present challenges in hyperspectral image analysis, and using wavelets to address this directly in a neural network super-resolution task is a good approach.

    *   **LFSE, HFSE, and HLFD Modules:** These modules, while designed for this specific architecture, might borrow concepts from other similar architectures (CNNs or Transformers) in vision. The extent to which these are specifically tailored *and* optimized for the Mamba architecture and the SHSR task is crucial for justifying their novelty. The description of these modules in the paper suggests a degree of novelty, as they leverage both channel attention and VSSM in unique ways.

*   **Significance:**

    *   **Performance:** The experimental results demonstrate state-of-the-art performance, which is a significant outcome.  The clear improvements over existing methods on multiple datasets and scale factors supports the effectiveness of the design choices.
    *   **Computational Efficiency:** The paper highlights the computational efficiency of HSRMamba. Showing that the proposed method reduces computation complexity while outperforming the second best method, MSDFormer, indicates that the improvements are not solely due to more computation.
    *   **Impact on the Field:** Hyperspectral image super-resolution is a relevant and important area.  Efficient and effective SHSR methods have the potential to significantly benefit various applications (e.g., remote sensing, precision agriculture). The use of Mamba, a relatively new architecture, in SHSR is also important.
    *   **Clarity:** The paper is well-written and the method is explained in a clear manner.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Architectural Exploration:** While the authors compare HSRMamba with state-of-the-art methods, it seems like there has not been much consideration for other wavelet-based approaches or further architectural modifications for the Mamba.
    *   **Ablation Study Limitations:** The ablation study is useful, but a deeper analysis of the influence of different wavelet bases, decomposition levels, and fusion techniques could have been added for better understanding.

**Justification for the Score:**

The paper presents a novel SHSR method with clear performance advantages and improved computational efficiency. The use of wavelet decomposition to address modal conflicts is a good approach, and the stripe-based scanning method represents a valuable adaptation of Visual Mamba. The experimental results strongly support the effectiveness of HSRMamba. However, there are some limits in architectural exploration and further analysis could have strengthened the work.

### Time Travel is Cheating: Going Live with DeepFund for Real-Time Fund Investment Benchmarking
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.11065v1)
- **Authors**: Changlun Li, Yao Shi, Chen Wang, Qiqi Duan, Runke Ruan, Weijie Huang, Haonan Long, Lijun Huang, Yuyu Luo, Nan Tang
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) have demonstrated notable capabilities across financial tasks, including financial report summarization, earnings call transcript analysis, and asset classification. However, their real-world effectiveness in managing complex fund investment remains inadequately assessed. A fundamental limitation of existing benchmarks for evaluating LLM-driven trading strategies is their reliance on historical back-testing, inadvertently enabling LLMs to "time travel"-leveraging future information embedded in their training corpora, thus resulting in possible information leakage and overly optimistic performance estimates. To address this issue, we introduce DeepFund, a live fund benchmark tool designed to rigorously evaluate LLM in real-time market conditions. Utilizing a multi-agent architecture, DeepFund connects directly with real-time stock market data-specifically data published after each model pretraining cutoff-to ensure fair and leakage-free evaluations. Empirical tests on nine flagship LLMs from leading global institutions across multiple investment dimensions-including ticker-level analysis, investment decision-making, portfolio management, and risk control-reveal significant practical challenges. Notably, even cutting-edge models such as DeepSeek-V3 and Claude-3.7-Sonnet incur net trading losses within DeepFund real-time evaluation environment, underscoring the present limitations of LLMs for active fund management. Our code is available at https://github.com/HKUSTDial/DeepFund.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces DeepFund, a live fund benchmark tool designed to evaluate Large Language Models (LLMs) in real-time market conditions. The core motivation is to address the "time travel" problem in traditional backtesting, where LLMs may inadvertently leverage future information from their training data, leading to inflated performance estimates. DeepFund connects directly with real-time stock market data (specifically data published *after* each model's pretraining cutoff) and uses a multi-agent architecture to simulate realistic fund management processes. The paper presents empirical results on nine flagship LLMs, highlighting the practical challenges and limitations of using LLMs for active fund management, as even advanced models often incur net trading losses in this live environment.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper addresses a crucial limitation in the application of LLMs to financial modeling: the risk of information leakage through backtesting. This "time travel" problem is well-recognized, but the paper provides a concrete solution by creating a live benchmark, which is a significant step forward.

*   **Novelty:**  The most novel aspect is the development and deployment of DeepFund as a *live* benchmark. While simulations of trading strategies are common, the focus on real-time data and a structured multi-agent framework to prevent information leakage sets this work apart. The multi-agent architecture, simulating different roles within a fund, adds a degree of realism that many prior studies lack.
*   **Significance:** The findings are significant because they challenge the overly optimistic performance estimates often reported for LLMs in finance. Showing that even state-of-the-art models struggle to achieve profitability in a live trading environment is a sobering result. The analysis of different trading personalities exhibited by various LLMs is also valuable.  This highlights the potential benefits of different AI agents in diverse market scenarios. These findings have important implications for researchers and practitioners considering the use of LLMs in real-world financial applications. Furthermore, the provided code makes the research more transparent and accessible for further investigation.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem definition and motivation.
    *   Well-defined and implemented benchmark.
    *   Emphasis on live, leakage-free evaluation.
    *   Analysis of LLM trading behaviors.
    *   Open-source code.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Trading Period:** The trading period (March 17 to April 17, 2025) is relatively short and might not be representative of all market conditions.  The period was defined by two specific events, but this means that broader economic conditions are not factored in.
    *   **Simplified Trading Process:** The paper acknowledges that the trading process is simplified and doesn't account for factors like transaction fees, market impact, or sophisticated trading strategies. These simplifications may limit the generalizability of the results.
    *   **Portfolio Configuration:** Restricting the portfolio to only Berkshire Hathaway's top 5 holdings, may also limit the conclusions that can be drawn regarding the LLMs capacity to adapt to a variety of investment portfolios.
    *   The choice of a fixed risk-free rate seems somewhat arbitrary, and perhaps the inclusion of varying values could enrich the investigation.

While the benchmark is simplified, it’s a crucial first step. Future work could expand the trading period, incorporate additional market considerations and investment classes, and test more complex investment strategies. Despite these weaknesses, the paper presents a solid contribution to the field, directly tackling a significant challenge in AI-driven finance. The creation of DeepFund as a real-time benchmarking tool represents tangible progress, enabling more rigorous and realistic evaluations of LLMs for financial applications.

### Towards Self-Improvement of Diffusion Models via Group Preference Optimization
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.11070v1)
- **Authors**: Renjie Chen, Wenfeng Lin, Yichen Zhang, Jiangchuan Wei, Boyuan Liu, Chao Feng, Jiao Ran, Mingyu Guo
- **Abstract**: Aligning text-to-image (T2I) diffusion models with Direct Preference Optimization (DPO) has shown notable improvements in generation quality. However, applying DPO to T2I faces two challenges: the sensitivity of DPO to preference pairs and the labor-intensive process of collecting and annotating high-quality data. In this work, we demonstrate that preference pairs with marginal differences can degrade DPO performance. Since DPO relies exclusively on relative ranking while disregarding the absolute difference of pairs, it may misclassify losing samples as wins, or vice versa. We empirically show that extending the DPO from pairwise to groupwise and incorporating reward standardization for reweighting leads to performance gains without explicit data selection. Furthermore, we propose Group Preference Optimization (GPO), an effective self-improvement method that enhances performance by leveraging the model's own capabilities without requiring external data. Extensive experiments demonstrate that GPO is effective across various diffusion models and tasks. Specifically, combining with widely used computer vision models, such as YOLO and OCR, the GPO improves the accurate counting and text rendering capabilities of the Stable Diffusion 3.5 Medium by 20 percentage points. Notably, as a plug-and-play method, no extra overhead is introduced during inference.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Towards Self-Improvement of Diffusion Models via Group Preference Optimization" addresses limitations in aligning text-to-image (T2I) diffusion models using Direct Preference Optimization (DPO). It identifies that DPO is sensitive to the quality of preference pairs and that marginal differences in preference can degrade performance. The authors propose Group Preference Optimization (GPO), which extends DPO from pairwise comparisons to groupwise comparisons and incorporates reward standardization. GPO allows for self-improvement by leveraging the model's own generated data for training, bypassing the need for external datasets. The authors demonstrate that GPO improves performance across various diffusion models and tasks, including accurate object counting and text rendering.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in the GPO formulation. While DPO has been applied to T2I models, the extension to groupwise preference optimization with reward standardization is a meaningful contribution. The self-improvement aspect is also valuable, reducing reliance on expensive, human-annotated preference data. The insight that marginal preference pairs can undermine DPO's performance is also a novel and valuable contribution to the field.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a practical and crucial problem: the reliance on high-quality preference data for aligning diffusion models. GPO's ability to enable self-improvement without external data has the potential to lower the barrier to entry for fine-tuning diffusion models and could lead to more adaptable and controllable image generation. The improvements demonstrated in tasks like accurate counting and text rendering are practically relevant, especially given the current limitations of many T2I models.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper provides a clear and well-motivated problem statement.
    *   The GPO formulation is mathematically sound and intuitively explained.
    *   The experiments are thorough and cover a range of diffusion models, tasks, and ablations.
    *   The results demonstrate significant improvements over baseline methods, especially in accurate counting and text rendering.
    *   The paper is well-written and easy to follow.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the experiments cover different models, there is a lot of focus on SD3.5M, and it's not clear how well the approach would scale to the very latest models (as of the writing of the paper).
    *   The discussion of model collapse is brief and lacks a deep analysis of the potential risks and mitigation strategies. While they say that small learning rate mitigates the problem, it isn't clear how sensitive the approach is to model collapse.
    *   The section on limitations discusses the inherent constraint of the base model and the increased computational cost. Future work should consider how GPO could be extended to allow models to improve features which are not possible by using current methods. This opens the opportunity to explore a wider range of options.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field by providing a more data-efficient and self-sufficient method for aligning diffusion models. GPO could be adopted by practitioners looking to fine-tune their models for specific tasks or to improve certain capabilities without relying on extensive human annotation. This opens up possibilities for continuous model improvement and personalization.

*   **Rigorous Rationale:** The paper clearly identifies a limitation in current DPO approaches for T2I models and provides a solution that addresses this issue effectively. The gains are significant, especially given that the results were generated with online generation of data without extra external data. It does suffer from the limitations mentioned above, meaning there is some room for improvements and further experiments.

### Addition is almost all you need: Compressing neural networks with double binary factorization
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.11076v1)
- **Authors**: Vladimír Boža, Vladimír Macko
- **Abstract**: Binary quantization approaches, which replace weight matrices with binary matrices and substitute costly multiplications with cheaper additions, offer a computationally efficient approach to address the increasing computational and storage requirements of Large Language Models (LLMs). However, the severe quantization constraint ($\pm1$) can lead to significant accuracy degradation. In this paper, we propose Double Binary Factorization (DBF), a novel method that factorizes dense weight matrices into products of two binary (sign) matrices, each accompanied by scaling vectors. DBF preserves the efficiency advantages of binary representations while achieving compression rates that are competitive with or superior to state-of-the-art methods. Specifically, in a 1-bit per weight range, DBF is better than existing binarization approaches. In a 2-bit per weight range, DBF is competitive with the best quantization methods like QuIP\# and QTIP. Unlike most existing compression techniques, which offer limited compression level choices, DBF allows fine-grained control over compression ratios by adjusting the factorization's intermediate dimension. Based on this advantage, we further introduce an algorithm for estimating non-uniform layer-wise compression ratios for DBF, based on previously developed channel pruning criteria. Code available at: https://github.com/usamec/double_binary
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Double Binary Factorization (DBF), a novel method for compressing neural networks, particularly Large Language Models (LLMs). DBF factorizes dense weight matrices into the product of two binary (sign) matrices, each scaled by a vector. This approach maintains the computational efficiency benefits of binary representations (replacing multiplications with additions) while achieving competitive compression rates compared to state-of-the-art quantization techniques.  DBF also allows for fine-grained control over compression ratios and incorporates input/output importance scaling during factorization. An iterative pruning algorithm is also proposed to dynamically allocate compression budgets across layers. The method is evaluated on Llama2-7B and Llama3-8B, showing improved performance over single-matrix binarization and competitiveness with other quantization methods, along with measured speedups.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:**  The core idea of factorizing a weight matrix into *two* binary matrices instead of one, with scaling vectors, is a worthwhile incremental advance. The connection to the authors' previous work on Double Sparse Factorization is clear.  The idea of using the factorization's middle dimension as channels for applying channel pruning criteria is also reasonably novel. However, the individual components (binary quantization, matrix factorization, channel pruning) are all well-established, reducing the overall novelty.

* **Significance:**  The paper addresses a crucial problem in the field: reducing the computational and storage costs of LLMs.  DBF offers a potentially practical solution that leverages binary arithmetic for efficiency.  The experimental results demonstrate that DBF achieves compression rates comparable to existing quantization methods while maintaining reasonable accuracy. While AQLM and QTIP often outperform DBF, the advantage of using just additions for inference with DBF is an important advantage that can lead to very efficient implementations. The measured speedups reinforce the practical potential. It also addresses a limitation with some other quantization methods, which only allow for integer numbers of bits per weight.

* **Strengths:**
    * Clear and well-written explanation of the method.
    * Comprehensive experimental evaluation on standard LLMs.
    * Demonstrated speedups on GPU hardware.
    * Addresses a practical problem with a computationally efficient solution.
    * The paper emphasizes the flexibility in compression ratios.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * The gains in perplexity and accuracy are often marginal compared to the best quantization methods, particularly when PV-tuning is used, meaning fine-tuning the continuous parameters improves most of the DBF methods.
    * Limited exploration of different network architectures and datasets.
    * While the paper emphasizes flexibility of compression ratios, little analysis of this tradeoff is presented.
    * The improvement from iterative pruning for non-uniform layer compression is modest.
    * Fine-tuning binary matrices using straight-through estimators or PV-tuning, which are used in conjunction with DBF, add complexity and potentially negate some of the energy efficiency gains of binarization.
    * There is no theoretical analysis, such as proving error bounds or convergence. The justification of the algorithm for computing the factorization is purely empirical.

* **Impact:**  The paper has the potential to influence the field by offering a new approach to LLM compression that balances accuracy and efficiency. The emphasis on binary arithmetic could lead to hardware-accelerated implementations and energy savings. However, to make a major impact, DBF would need to consistently outperform other leading quantization methods, or offer some other substantial advantage.

* **Justification:** DBF is a useful addition to the LLM compression toolbox. It's a well-executed approach based on solid principles and demonstrating promising results. The major limitation is that other quantization techniques already offer similar or better accuracy. While the "addition-only" arithmetic and flexibility are compelling, these advantages need to be more decisively demonstrated.

### LLM-Enhanced Symbolic Control for Safety-Critical Applications
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.11077v1)
- **Authors**: Amir Bayat, Alessandro Abate, Necmiye Ozay, Raphaël M. Jungers
- **Abstract**: Motivated by Smart Manufacturing and Industry 4.0, we introduce a framework for synthesizing Abstraction-Based Controller Design (ABCD) for reach-avoid problems from Natural Language (NL) specifications using Large Language Models (LLMs). A Code Agent interprets an NL description of the control problem and translates it into a formal language interpretable by state-of-the-art symbolic control software, while a Checker Agent verifies the correctness of the generated code and enhances safety by identifying specification mismatches. Evaluations show that the system handles linguistic variability and improves robustness over direct planning with LLMs. The proposed approach lowers the barrier to formal control synthesis by enabling intuitive, NL-based task definition while maintaining safety guarantees through automated validation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a framework that leverages Large Language Models (LLMs) to automate the process of Abstraction-Based Controller Design (ABCD) for safety-critical applications.  The framework uses a "Code Agent" to translate Natural Language (NL) specifications of control problems into formal code interpretable by ABCD tools like Dionysos. A "Checker Agent" then validates this generated code, identifies errors and mismatches against the original NL specification, and provides feedback for refinement.  The authors evaluate their approach on a benchmark dataset of reach-avoid problems, demonstrating improved robustness and correctness compared to direct planning with LLMs, while still maintaining safety guarantees through automated validation.  The key benefit is to lower the barrier for non-expert users to utilize formal control synthesis by using intuitive NL task definitions.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a reasonable level of novelty by combining LLMs with symbolic control techniques. Using LLMs for code generation isn't entirely new, and neither is ABCD. The novelty lies in specifically tailoring and integrating LLMs within an ABCD pipeline to *directly* address the interface challenge of translating NL requirements to formal control synthesis. The introduction of a "Checker Agent" to specifically validate the generated code further enhances the novelty, adding a layer of safety and reliability. However, other works explore LLMs for formal specification synthesis (temporal logic) and code generation for different aspects of robotics. The specific integration with Dionysos and the two-agent (Code/Checker) architecture is the key novelty.

*   **Significance:** The potential significance of this work lies in broadening the adoption of formal control methods. Making formal methods more accessible through NL interfaces can democratize their use, particularly in areas like smart manufacturing where diverse operators need to define control tasks. The evaluation, while limited, indicates a tangible improvement in correctness and robustness, suggesting a real-world benefit. However, the results show an overall success rate of around 65%, indicating that significant further research is needed to bring the approach to a state suitable for practical application.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Problem Addressed:** Targets a key challenge in formal methods: bridging the gap between human intuition and machine-interpretable specifications.
    *   **Framework Design:**  The two-agent (Code/Checker) architecture is a sensible design, leveraging LLMs' strengths while mitigating their inherent unreliability with a validation step.
    *   **Demonstrated Improvement:** The experimental results show a clear advantage of the proposed framework over direct LLM planning, particularly in terms of correctness and avoiding unsafe behavior.
    *   **Practical Motivation:** The focus on Industry 4.0 and smart manufacturing provides a clear practical motivation and target application.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scope:**  The evaluation focuses exclusively on reach-avoid problems with known system dynamics. It remains unclear how well the framework would generalize to more complex control problems (e.g., hybrid systems, stochastic systems) or situations where the system dynamics are uncertain or changing.
    *   **Evaluation Size:** The dataset of 20 problems, though paraphrased, is relatively small. A larger, more diverse dataset would provide a more robust assessment of the framework's capabilities.
    *   **LLM Limitations:** The performance is inherently limited by the capabilities and biases of the underlying LLM.  The paper acknowledges this but doesn't fully explore potential strategies for mitigating these limitations beyond few-shot prompting. The limitations of grounding only in text are very prominent.
    *   **Lack of Formal Guarantees:** While the Checker Agent improves reliability, it doesn't provide formal guarantees of correctness. The final synthesized controller relies on the soundness of the ABCD framework, but the LLM-generated code itself isn't formally verified.

*   **Impact:** The impact of this work is contingent on further development and validation. If the framework can be scaled to handle more complex systems and provide stronger guarantees, it could significantly impact how formal control methods are deployed in various domains. As it currently stands, the impact is limited due to the noted weaknesses.
*   **Missing information:** The specific details about the implementation of the LLM agents (specific prompts used beyond examples shown, the specifics of the LLM API interaction, costs and timings for running the pipeline) are glossed over. This makes replication and further exploration more difficult.
**Justification for Score:**

Given the reasonable novelty in combining LLMs with symbolic control using a two-agent architecture, the demonstration of improved robustness, and the significance of addressing the interface challenge in formal methods, but considering the limitations in scope, evaluation size, lack of formal guarantees on the LLM component, and the dependence on LLM performance, the paper merits a score reflecting a promising but still early-stage contribution.
