### $\mathcal{A}LLM4ADD$: Unlocking the Capabilities of Audio Large Language Models for Audio Deepfake Detection
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.11079v1)
- **Authors**: Hao Gu, Jiangyan Yi, Chenglong Wang, Jianhua Tao, Zheng Lian, Jiayi He, Yong Ren, Yujie Chen, Zhengqi Wen
- **Abstract**: Audio deepfake detection (ADD) has grown increasingly important due to the rise of high-fidelity audio generative models and their potential for misuse. Given that audio large language models (ALLMs) have made significant progress in various audio processing tasks, a heuristic question arises: Can ALLMs be leveraged to solve ADD?. In this paper, we first conduct a comprehensive zero-shot evaluation of ALLMs on ADD, revealing their ineffectiveness in detecting fake audio. To enhance their performance, we propose $\mathcal{A}LLM4ADD$, an ALLM-driven framework for ADD. Specifically, we reformulate ADD task as an audio question answering problem, prompting the model with the question: "Is this audio fake or real?". We then perform supervised fine-tuning to enable the ALLM to assess the authenticity of query audio. Extensive experiments are conducted to demonstrate that our ALLM-based method can achieve superior performance in fake audio detection, particularly in data-scarce scenarios. As a pioneering study, we anticipate that this work will inspire the research community to leverage ALLMs to develop more effective ADD systems.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "ALLM4ADD: Unlocking the Capabilities of Audio Large Language Models for Audio Deepfake Detection":

**Summary:**

The paper explores the potential of Audio Large Language Models (ALLMs) for audio deepfake detection (ADD).  The authors first demonstrate that existing ALLMs perform poorly in zero-shot ADD.  To address this, they introduce ALLM4ADD, a framework that reformulates ADD as an Audio Question Answering (AQA) task. The framework fine-tunes ALLMs by prompting them with questions like "Is this audio fake or real?",  leading to improved performance, especially in data-scarce scenarios. The paper provides extensive experiments showcasing the superiority of the ALLM-based approach compared to conventional pipeline and end-to-end models.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of directly applying ALLMs to ADD is relatively novel. Prior works have focused on traditional signal processing techniques or end-to-end deep learning models. The reformulation of ADD as an AQA task is a clever way to leverage the existing capabilities of ALLMs. However, the paper is not breaking completely new ground. Using LLMs/VLMMs for similar tasks (e.g. fake image detection using zero-shot and few-shot learning) is already somewhat well-established, so the core concept is an adaptation rather than a radical innovation. A stronger emphasis on the specific challenges of audio and the advantages of ALLMs in this particular modality could have significantly improved the paper's novelty.

*   **Significance:** The significance of the work lies in demonstrating that ALLMs can be effectively adapted for ADD.  This opens up new avenues for research in this critical area, leveraging the rapid advancements in LLMs. The improved performance, especially in data-scarce scenarios, is a valuable contribution as it addresses a major challenge in deepfake detection. The paper provides a clear methodology and thorough experimental results which are valuable to the community. This is crucial because the performance on the ASVspoof 2019 dataset is approaching saturation so new methods or datasets are needed to push the research forward.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper is well-written and clearly explains the proposed method.
    *   The experimental setup is comprehensive, with a wide range of baselines and evaluation metrics.
    *   The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the impact of different design choices.
    *   The evaluation in data-scarce scenarios is particularly relevant.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The paper could have explored more sophisticated prompting strategies to further improve performance.
    *   The explanation of the failure of zero-shot ALLMs could be more detailed.  Why are they failing and what is it about this modality that makes it difficult?
    *   The paper could benefit from more in-depth analysis of the ALLM's learned representations and how they contribute to fake audio detection. How is this feature space different from the raw audio?
    *   The performance on the In-the-Wild dataset, while superior to baselines, indicates that the model still faces challenges in real-world scenarios. This weakness should be discussed and further mitigated in future research.

*   **Impact:** The paper is likely to inspire further research on using ALLMs for ADD.  It provides a solid foundation for developing more effective and robust deepfake detection systems. It may also encourage the exploration of similar approaches in other audio processing tasks.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:**

The paper provides a valuable contribution to the field by demonstrating the potential of ALLMs for audio deepfake detection. The reformulation of ADD as an AQA task and the ALLM4ADD framework are novel and effective. The comprehensive experimental results and ablation studies are strong points. However, the incremental novelty (adaptation of LLMs to a similar task) and room for improvement in prompt engineering and analysis of learned representations prevent the paper from achieving a higher score. Also, a clear delineation of how the presented work advances the overall field of ALLMs could strengthen the assessment. It opens the door to using ALLMs for audio deepfake detection, which makes this a worthwhile and valuable effort.


- **Classification**: cs.SD
- **Score**: 7/10

### ShiQ: Bringing back Bellman to LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.11081v1)
- **Authors**: Pierre Clavier, Nathan Grinsztajn, Raphael Avalos, Yannis Flet-Berliac, Irem Ergun, Omar D. Domingues, Eugene Tarassov, Olivier Pietquin, Pierre H. Richemond, Florian Strub, Matthieu Geist
- **Abstract**: The fine-tuning of pre-trained large language models (LLMs) using reinforcement learning (RL) is generally formulated as direct policy optimization. This approach was naturally favored as it efficiently improves a pretrained LLM, seen as an initial policy. Another RL paradigm, Q-learning methods, has received far less attention in the LLM community while demonstrating major success in various non-LLM RL tasks. In particular, Q-learning effectiveness comes from its sample efficiency and ability to learn offline, which is particularly valuable given the high computational cost of sampling with LLMs. However, naively applying a Q-learning-style update to the model's logits is ineffective due to the specificity of LLMs. Our core contribution is to derive theoretically grounded loss functions from Bellman equations to adapt Q-learning methods to LLMs. To do so, we carefully adapt insights from the RL literature to account for LLM-specific characteristics, ensuring that the logits become reliable Q-value estimates. We then use this loss to build a practical algorithm, ShiQ for Shifted-Q, that supports off-policy, token-wise learning while remaining simple to implement. Finally, we evaluate ShiQ on both synthetic data and real-world benchmarks, e.g., UltraFeedback and BFCL-V3, demonstrating its effectiveness in both single-turn and multi-turn LLM settings
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "ShiQ: Bringing back Bellman to LLMs" addresses the problem of reinforcement learning (RL) fine-tuning of large language models (LLMs). It argues that while policy-gradient methods like PPO are dominant, Q-learning approaches have been relatively neglected in the LLM space despite their advantages in sample efficiency and offline learning.  The paper's core contribution is the derivation of theoretically grounded loss functions based on Bellman equations, specifically tailored to the characteristics of LLMs. This results in a practical algorithm, ShiQ (Shifted-Q), that supports off-policy, token-wise learning while being relatively simple to implement. The authors demonstrate ShiQ's effectiveness on both synthetic data and real-world benchmarks (UltraFeedback, BFCL-V3), showing improvements in single-turn and multi-turn LLM scenarios.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a novel adaptation of Q-learning to LLMs. While Q-learning itself is not new, its successful application and adaptation to the specific challenges of LLMs are original. The theoretical grounding through Bellman equation derivations and the address of LLM-specific issues (computational cost, initialization problems, and sequence-level rewards) significantly strengthens the approach. The shift from relying on raw Q values to learning a shift relative to the reference logits is a notable practical contribution. The derivation of the consistency equations leading to the algorithm, and the individual ways they address LLM's specificities are all significant.
*   **Significance:**  The work has the potential to be significant for the following reasons:
    *   *Sample efficiency:*  Q-learning generally offers better sample efficiency than policy gradients, which is crucial given the cost of sampling from LLMs.
    *   *Offline learning:* ShiQ can effectively learn from existing datasets, which is relevant in scenarios with limited online interaction.
    *   *Multi-turn capability:* ShiQ's good performance in the BFCL-V3 benchmark demonstrates its ability to handle multi-turn interactions and reason over longer sequences of rewards, a common problem area for other RL methods.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   *Theoretical foundation:* The paper provides a strong theoretical justification for the proposed approach, based on Bellman equations and a careful adaptation of existing RL concepts.
    *   *LLM-specific design:*  The algorithm addresses key challenges related to applying Q-learning to LLMs, leading to a practical and efficient implementation.
    *   *Empirical validation:*  The paper presents comprehensive experimental results on synthetic data and real-world benchmarks, demonstrating the effectiveness of the proposed approach. The ablation studies also highlight the importance of the different components.
    *   *Clear writing and structure:* The paper is well-structured and clearly written, making it accessible to a broad audience.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   *Reward model dependence:*  ShiQ relies on a reward model, which can be a limitation if the reward model is flawed or biased. The paper acknowledges this and suggests it may be important to address these issues in future work.
    *   *Limited scope of evaluation:* There are many potential directions for future evaluation, which include broader benchmark. Also the approach could benefit from applying this on downstream benchmarks and robotics.

    *   *Potential limited adoption:* The algorithm is quite specific and has to take into account specifics of LLM that make general adoption harder.

*   **Potential Influence:** This paper has the potential to influence the field of LLM fine-tuning by:
    *   *Providing a viable alternative to policy gradient methods.*
    *   *Encouraging more research into Q-learning-based approaches for LLMs.*
    *   *Inspiring new algorithms and techniques for addressing the challenges of RL fine-tuning of LLMs.*

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel and theoretically grounded approach to RL fine-tuning of LLMs, addressing key challenges in the field and demonstrating promising empirical results. The limitations are well-acknowledged, and the potential for further research is clear.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 8/10

### Towards Better Evaluation for Generated Patent Claims
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.11095v1)
- **Authors**: Lekang Jiang, Pascal A Scherz, Stephan Goetz
- **Abstract**: Patent claims define the scope of protection and establish the legal boundaries of an invention. Drafting these claims is a complex and time-consuming process that usually requires the expertise of skilled patent attorneys, which can form a large access barrier for many small enterprises. To solve these challenges, researchers have investigated the use of large language models (LLMs) for automating patent claim generation. However, existing studies highlight inconsistencies between automated evaluation metrics and human expert assessments. To bridge this gap, we introduce Patent-CE, the first comprehensive benchmark for evaluating patent claims. Patent-CE includes comparative claim evaluations annotated by patent experts, focusing on five key criteria: feature completeness, conceptual clarity, terminology consistency, logical linkage, and overall quality. Additionally, we propose PatClaimEval, a novel multi-dimensional evaluation method specifically designed for patent claims. Our experiments demonstrate that PatClaimEval achieves the highest correlation with human expert evaluations across all assessment criteria among all tested metrics. This research provides the groundwork for more accurate evaluations of automated patent claim generation systems.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of reliably evaluating automatically generated patent claims. It introduces Patent-CE, the first comprehensive benchmark dataset for this task, comprising comparative evaluations of patent claims annotated by patent experts based on feature completeness, conceptual clarity, terminology consistency, logical linkage, and overall quality. The authors also propose PatClaimEval, a novel multi-dimensional evaluation method for patent claims, leveraging the Longformer architecture and contrastive learning.  Experiments demonstrate that PatClaimEval achieves higher correlations with human expert evaluations than existing metrics across all assessment criteria.  The work aims to pave the way for more accurate assessments of automated patent claim generation systems.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The creation of Patent-CE is a significant contribution.  Existing benchmarks for text generation evaluation are often unsuitable for the specific requirements of patent claims, which demand technical accuracy and adherence to legal standards. Patent-CE fills this gap by providing a curated dataset with expert annotations tailored to the nuances of patent claim evaluation. This is a substantial and well-defined improvement over existing approaches. The idea of using comparative evaluation format instead of rating the quality independently is a good idea, which can greatly reduce the annotation costs while still keeps the evaluation quality.

*   **Significance:**  The ability to accurately evaluate generated patent claims is crucial for the development of automated patent claim generation systems. This research provides a crucial tool for researchers to develop and refine claim generation models. The limitations of current automated evaluation metrics have been highlighted in prior work, and this research offers a more reliable alternative. This could accelerate research in this area, potentially lowering costs and improving access to patent protection for smaller entities. PatClaimEval, with its multi-dimensional approach and contrastive learning, is a meaningful contribution that can benefit both patent attorneys and AI scientists.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Comprehensive Benchmark: Patent-CE is a valuable resource with diverse data and expert annotations. The dataset size, while not enormous, is adequate and likely costly to create given the expert annotation requirement.
    *   Effective Evaluation Method: PatClaimEval demonstrates superior performance compared to established metrics, highlighting its suitability for patent claim evaluation.
    *   Well-Defined Metrics: The evaluation criteria (completeness, clarity, consistency, linkage, and quality) are clearly defined and grounded in patent examination standards.
    *   Thorough Experiments: The paper includes a solid experimental setup comparing PatClaimEval to a wide range of baselines.
    *   Qualitative Analysis: The inclusion of qualitative examples strengthens the paper by illustrating the limitations of existing metrics and showcasing the advantages of PatClaimEval.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   English-Only: The dataset is limited to English patents, potentially affecting its generalizability to other languages and legal systems. While understandable given the resource constraints, this should be acknowledged.
    *   Reference-Based Evaluation: The reliance on reference claims for evaluation restricts the ability to assess novelty and non-obviousness, which are core criteria in real-world patent examinations. The authors acknowledge this in the limitations section, but it's a fundamental constraint.
    *   Scalability/Complexity of PatClaimEval: Although PatClaimEval performs well, the paper could benefit from a more detailed analysis of its computational complexity and scalability, which might be a concern for very large-scale evaluation.
    *   Limited model comparison/experiments: it would be more valuable if the authors evaluate the effectiveness of Patent-CE in assisting with the selection of patent claim generation model.

*   **Potential Influence:** This work has the potential to significantly influence the field of automated patent claim generation. Patent-CE can serve as a standard benchmark for evaluating new models and methods. PatClaimEval can be adopted as a more reliable evaluation metric, guiding the development of more effective claim generation systems. This will have practical implications for reducing the costs and increasing the efficiency of the patent process.

*   **Overall:** The paper presents a novel and significant contribution to the field. The creation of the Patent-CE benchmark and the development of PatClaimEval address a critical need for reliable evaluation metrics in automated patent claim generation. While some limitations exist, the strengths of the research outweigh the weaknesses.

**Score: 7.5**

**Justification:** The paper is novel and important, but it is not groundbreaking. Its value lies in the practical contribution of a specialized dataset and evaluation method. The limitations related to reference-based evaluation and the English-only scope slightly reduce the overall impact. More studies of the application for model selection can make the contribution more valuable. However, the clarity of presentation, thorough experimental evaluation, and potential for significant influence in the domain of patent AI warrant a high score.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Hybrid-Emba3D: Geometry-Aware and Cross-Path Feature Hybrid Enhanced State Space Model for Point Cloud Classification
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.11099v1)
- **Authors**: Bin Liu, Chunyang Wang, Xuelian Liu, Guan Xi, Ge Zhang, Ziteng Yao, Mengxue Dong
- **Abstract**: The point cloud classification tasks face the dual challenge of efficiently extracting local geometric features while maintaining model complexity. The Mamba architecture utilizes the linear complexity advantage of state space models (SSMs) to overcome the computational bottleneck of Transformers while balancing global modeling capabilities. However, the inherent contradiction between its unidirectional dependency and the unordered nature of point clouds impedes modeling spatial correlation in local neighborhoods, thus constraining geometric feature extraction. This paper proposes Hybrid-Emba3D, a bidirectional Mamba model enhanced by geometry-feature coupling and cross-path feature hybridization. The Local geometric pooling with geometry-feature coupling mechanism significantly enhances local feature discriminative power via coordinated propagation and dynamic aggregation of geometric information between local center points and their neighborhoods, without introducing additional parameters. The designed Collaborative feature enhancer adopts dual-path hybridization, effectively handling local mutations and sparse key signals, breaking through the limitations of traditional SSM long-range modeling. Experimental results demonstrate that the proposed model achieves a new SOTA classification accuracy of 95.99% on ModelNet40 with only 0.03M additional.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Hybrid-Emba3D, a novel architecture for point cloud classification built upon the Mamba state space model (SSM). It addresses the challenge of balancing local geometric feature extraction with efficient long-range dependency modeling in point clouds.  Hybrid-Emba3D incorporates two key components: a Geometry-Aware Local Pooling (LGP) mechanism that couples geometric features between center points and their neighborhoods, and a Collaborative Feature Enhancer (CoFE) within a Bidirectional SSM (BiSSM) that promotes global context awareness through multi-scale feature fusion. The paper claims state-of-the-art results on ModelNet40 and demonstrates improved performance on ScanObjectNN, particularly in challenging scenarios. The authors emphasize the lightweight nature of their modifications and provide ablation studies to validate the contribution of each component.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a relatively incremental improvement over existing Mamba-based architectures for point cloud processing. While the combination of LGP and CoFE-BiSSM is novel, the individual components are inspired by prior work on geometric feature extraction and bidirectional modeling. The novelty primarily lies in adapting these components to the Mamba architecture in a specific way. The geometry coupling mechanism, while effective, isn't groundbreaking in its approach, echoing concepts from earlier geometric deep learning methods.
*   **Significance:** The paper's significance is tied to its performance gains and efficiency. Achieving a new SOTA on ModelNet40 is notable, especially with a small increase in parameters. The improved performance on ScanObjectNN, especially the PB_T50_RS variant, which simulates real-world challenges, indicates robustness. However, the ModelNet40 benchmark is considered saturated.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   *Strong Performance:* Demonstrates competitive and SOTA results on standard benchmarks.
    *   *Efficiency:* Presents a lightweight architecture with minimal additional parameters and computational overhead.
    *   *Ablation Studies:* Provides thorough ablation studies to demonstrate the contribution of each component.
    *   *Clear Writing:* The paper is generally well-written and organized.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   *Incremental Novelty:* The core ideas are adaptations of existing concepts.
    *   *Benchmark Saturation:*  Reliance on ModelNet40, which has diminishing returns, weakens the perceived impact of the SOTA result.
    *   *Limited Theoretical Analysis:* The paper lacks a deeper theoretical analysis of why the proposed components work well together and how the BiSSM specifically addresses limitations in the original Mamba architecture.
    *   *Few-shot Learning Results:* The few-shot learning results, while improved, don't consistently outperform the best existing methods, suggesting room for improvement in generalization.
    *   *Limited Discussion of Failure Cases:* While the authors mention dresser classification, a more comprehensive discussion of failure cases and limitations would strengthen the analysis.
*   **Potential Impact:**  The paper may influence the development of more efficient Mamba-based architectures for point cloud processing. The LGP and CoFE-BiSSM modules could be adopted or adapted in other contexts. However, given the incremental nature, its impact may be limited to a refinement of existing approaches rather than a paradigm shift.

**Justification for Score:**

I am assigning a score of 6 to this paper. While it demonstrates strong empirical performance and offers a lightweight architecture, the novelty is relatively incremental. The success depends on a specific combination of existing ideas adapted to the Mamba framework.  The heavy reliance on the ModelNet40 benchmark limits the perceived impact, and a deeper theoretical analysis is missing. While the architecture is efficient and provides good performance gains on several datasets, it is not a fundamentally new method, more an effective adaptation of Mamba to point clouds. The limited details around the failure mode analysis prevents a more refined score.

**Score: 6**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### Group Think: Multiple Concurrent Reasoning Agents Collaborating at Token Level Granularity
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.11107v1)
- **Authors**: Chan-Jan Hsu, Davide Buffelli, Jamie McGowan, Feng-Ting Liao, Yi-Chang Chen, Sattar Vakili, Da-shan Shiu
- **Abstract**: Recent advances in large language models (LLMs) have demonstrated the power of reasoning through self-generated chains of thought. Multiple reasoning agents can collaborate to raise joint reasoning quality above individual outcomes. However, such agents typically interact in a turn-based manner, trading increased latency for improved quality. In this paper, we propose Group Think--a single LLM that acts as multiple concurrent reasoning agents, or thinkers. With shared visibility into each other's partial generation progress, Group Think introduces a new concurrent-reasoning paradigm in which multiple reasoning trajectories adapt dynamically to one another at the token level. For example, a reasoning thread may shift its generation mid-sentence upon detecting that another thread is better positioned to continue. This fine-grained, token-level collaboration enables Group Think to reduce redundant reasoning and improve quality while achieving significantly lower latency. Moreover, its concurrent nature allows for efficient utilization of idle computational resources, making it especially suitable for edge inference, where very small batch size often underutilizes local~GPUs. We give a simple and generalizable modification that enables any existing LLM to perform Group Think on a local GPU. We also present an evaluation strategy to benchmark reasoning latency and empirically demonstrate latency improvements using open-source LLMs that were not explicitly trained for Group Think. We hope this work paves the way for future LLMs to exhibit more sophisticated and more efficient collaborative behavior for higher quality generation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the "Group Think: Multiple Concurrent Reasoning Agents Collaborating at Token Level Granularity" paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "Group Think," a novel approach to LLM inference where a single LLM acts as multiple concurrent reasoning agents (thinkers) that collaborate at the token level.  Unlike traditional multi-agent systems with turn-based communication, Group Think enables fine-grained collaboration where reasoning trajectories dynamically adapt to each other's progress.  The authors propose implementations that allow for efficient utilization of computational resources, particularly beneficial for edge inference scenarios with small batch sizes.  They demonstrate improved reasoning accuracy and reduced latency on various problems using open-source LLMs without explicit Group Think training.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of token-level concurrent reasoning is a significant departure from existing multi-agent and CoT techniques.  The novelty lies in the *granularity* of collaboration and the *concurrent* nature of the process, allowing for real-time adaptation between reasoning threads. It moves beyond simple parallel generation and turn-based interaction, offering a potentially more efficient and effective approach. However, some similarity is claimed from "Hogwild! Inference", which this work needs to highlight the difference.
*   **Significance:**  The potential impact of Group Think is substantial. First, by improving performance while also reducing inference latency, the approach has a good significance. Second, because it uses a single LLM without modification, it means the training can be done with relative ease. Given that even without explicit training, improvements are already observed is a good significance. Third, by improving compute efficiency this makes LLMs to be deployed on edge devices. Lastly, the method could potentially be a foundational step towards more sophisticated forms of collaborative AI, with LLMs functioning as coordinated problem-solving entities.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the limitations of existing approaches (latency in turn-based systems, redundancy in independent sampling) and positions Group Think as a solution.
    *   **Efficient Implementation:**  The proposed implementations, particularly the single-thread data center approach and the way they modify the attention mask, are practical and well-explained.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The experiments on diverse problem categories (enumeration, divide and conquer, coding) provide empirical evidence of the benefits of Group Think. The comparison to an independent sampling baseline effectively isolates the contribution of collaboration.
    *   **Practical Implications:** The emphasis on edge inference and the ability to retrofit existing LLMs are valuable for real-world applications.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Model Modification:** The models are not explicitly trained for Group Think. Therefore they are not fully exploiting all of its potential.
    *   **Simplistic Collaboration:** The in-context instruction is relatively simple. This might limit the complexity of the collaborative behaviors observed. Better in-context instructions could significantly change the observed behaviour, which make the results hard to generalize.
    *   **Evaluation Metrics:** While the use of coverage is appropriate, a more extensive analysis of solution quality (e.g., correctness, optimality, robustness) would strengthen the evaluation.
    *   **Lack of theoretical analysis.**

*   **Potential Influence:** This work has the potential to influence future research in several areas:

    *   **Multi-Agent LLM Systems:**  It offers a new paradigm for collaboration, moving beyond traditional agent architectures.
    *   **Efficient Inference Techniques:**  The implementation strategies could inspire other methods for optimizing LLM inference, especially on resource-constrained devices.
    *   **LLM Training:**  It suggests a direction for creating datasets and training models that are explicitly designed for collaborative reasoning.

*   **Justification for Score:** The paper presents a novel and promising approach to LLM inference, validated with solid empirical results and practical implementation strategies. However, the limited training and evaluation metrics prevent from being a top tier paper. Therefore it gets a
    **Score: 7**
- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### Deepfake Forensic Analysis: Source Dataset Attribution and Legal Implications of Synthetic Media Manipulation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.11110v1)
- **Authors**: Massimiliano Cassia, Luca Guarnera, Mirko Casu, Ignazio Zangara, Sebastiano Battiato
- **Abstract**: Synthetic media generated by Generative Adversarial Networks (GANs) pose significant challenges in verifying authenticity and tracing dataset origins, raising critical concerns in copyright enforcement, privacy protection, and legal compliance. This paper introduces a novel forensic framework for identifying the training dataset (e.g., CelebA or FFHQ) of GAN-generated images through interpretable feature analysis. By integrating spectral transforms (Fourier/DCT), color distribution metrics, and local feature descriptors (SIFT), our pipeline extracts discriminative statistical signatures embedded in synthetic outputs. Supervised classifiers (Random Forest, SVM, XGBoost) achieve 98-99% accuracy in binary classification (real vs. synthetic) and multi-class dataset attribution across diverse GAN architectures (StyleGAN, AttGAN, GDWCT, StarGAN, and StyleGAN2). Experimental results highlight the dominance of frequency-domain features (DCT/FFT) in capturing dataset-specific artifacts, such as upsampling patterns and spectral irregularities, while color histograms reveal implicit regularization strategies in GAN training. We further examine legal and ethical implications, showing how dataset attribution can address copyright infringement, unauthorized use of personal data, and regulatory compliance under frameworks like GDPR and California's AB 602. Our framework advances accountability and governance in generative modeling, with applications in digital forensics, content moderation, and intellectual property litigation.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper.

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a forensic framework for identifying the training dataset (CelebA or FFHQ) used to generate deepfake images created by various GAN architectures (StyleGAN, AttGAN, GDWCT, StarGAN, StyleGAN2). The framework combines spectral transforms (Fourier/DCT), color distribution metrics, and local feature descriptors (SIFT) to extract discriminative statistical signatures. Supervised classifiers (Random Forest, SVM, XGBoost) are then used to achieve high accuracy (98-99%) in binary classification (real vs. synthetic) and dataset attribution. The paper also discusses the legal and ethical implications of dataset attribution, focusing on copyright infringement, privacy violations, and regulatory compliance (GDPR, California's AB 602).

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Novelty in Focus:** The paper's primary strength lies in its specific focus on *dataset attribution* for deepfakes. While other research has focused on deepfake detection in general or architectural fingerprinting, this work explicitly attempts to identify the source dataset used for training a specific GAN model. This is a valuable distinction, especially in the context of legal and ethical considerations.
    *   **Methodological Approach:** The integration of multiple feature modalities (spectral transforms, color histograms, SIFT) is a solid strategy for capturing various dataset-specific artifacts. The use of common techniques (DCT, FFT, and SIFT) allows for a solid benchmark.
    *   **High Accuracy:** The reported accuracy (98-99%) on binary classification tasks is impressive. The results are also showing high F1 scores (the most useful for forensic work) in multi-modal cases.
    *   **Legal and Ethical Discussion:** The discussion of legal and ethical implications (copyright, privacy, GDPR) is timely and relevant, highlighting the practical importance of dataset attribution. This shows that the research is taking a step into the real world by addressing these important concerns.
    *   **Reproducibility:** The paper mentions using publicly available datasets (CelebA and FFHQ) and common GAN architectures, which enhances reproducibility.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Generalization:** The evaluation is confined to facial images generated from two specific datasets (CelebA and FFHQ) and five specific GAN architectures.  The generalizability of the framework to other types of images, datasets, and GAN models remains unclear. While the paper claims the approach is domain-agnostic, the experiments don't fully demonstrate this.
    *   **Lack of Robustness Testing:** The paper does not explicitly address the robustness of the framework against common image manipulations such as JPEG compression, resizing, or adversarial attacks. These manipulations are common in real-world scenarios and could significantly impact the accuracy of dataset attribution. Mentioning other studies, such as [12] is not enough if they are not tested and implemented in the framework.
    *   **Limited Interpretation of Spectral Features:** While the paper highlights the importance of frequency-domain features, the specific artifacts captured by DCT and FFT and their relationship to dataset characteristics are not thoroughly explained. A deeper analysis of the learned features could provide more insights into the inner workings of GANs and dataset biases.
    *   **Oversimplified Legal Analysis:** The legal analysis provides a general overview of relevant laws and regulations, but it lacks a detailed examination of the legal challenges associated with dataset attribution in specific scenarios (e.g., proving copyright infringement based on statistical signatures alone).

*   **Novelty and Significance:**
    *   The specific focus on dataset attribution (as opposed to general deepfake detection) is a novel contribution.
    *   The combination of interpretable features (spectral transforms, color histograms, SIFT) with supervised classifiers offers a practical approach for addressing this problem.
    *   The legal and ethical discussion highlights the potential impact of this research on synthetic media governance.

*   **Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a useful, well-executed, and timely study on dataset attribution for deepfake images. The high classification accuracy and the inclusion of legal and ethical considerations are noteworthy. However, the limited generalization, lack of robustness testing, limited interpretation of spectral features, and basic legal analysis prevents it from being a truly outstanding contribution. The paper is addressing relevant concerns and providing interesting data, but further research and work are required to give it more impact to the community.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Navigating the Alpha Jungle: An LLM-Powered MCTS Framework for Formulaic Factor Mining
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.11122v1)
- **Authors**: Yu Shi, Yitong Duan, Jian Li
- **Abstract**: Alpha factor mining is pivotal in quantitative investment for identifying predictive signals from complex financial data. While traditional formulaic alpha mining relies on human expertise, contemporary automated methods, such as those based on genetic programming or reinforcement learning, often suffer from search inefficiency or yield poorly interpretable alpha factors. This paper introduces a novel framework that integrates Large Language Models (LLMs) with Monte Carlo Tree Search (MCTS) to overcome these limitations. Our approach leverages the LLM's instruction-following and reasoning capability to iteratively generate and refine symbolic alpha formulas within an MCTS-driven exploration. A key innovation is the guidance of MCTS exploration by rich, quantitative feedback from financial backtesting of each candidate factor, enabling efficient navigation of the vast search space. Furthermore, a frequent subtree avoidance mechanism is introduced to bolster search efficiency and alpha factor performance. Experimental results on real-world stock market data demonstrate that our LLM-based framework outperforms existing methods by mining alphas with superior predictive accuracy, trading performance, and improved interpretability, while offering a more efficient solution for formulaic alpha mining.
- **Summary**: This paper introduces a novel framework for formulaic alpha mining, which combines Large Language Models (LLMs) with Monte Carlo Tree Search (MCTS). The approach leverages the LLM's reasoning and instruction-following capabilities to generate and refine symbolic alpha formulas within an MCTS-driven exploration process. A key innovation is the MCTS exploration guided by backtesting feedback of each candidate factor, facilitating efficient search in the vast alpha space. Furthermore, a frequent subtree avoidance mechanism is introduced to bolster search efficiency. Experimental results on stock market data demonstrate that the framework outperforms existing methods, yielding alphas with superior predictive accuracy, trading performance, and interpretability.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in the synergistic combination of LLMs and MCTS specifically tailored for formulaic alpha mining. While LLMs have been used in finance and MCTS has been applied to LLM reasoning tasks, the integration with quantitative feedback from financial backtesting to guide formula generation is a unique contribution. The frequent subtree avoidance further enhances the exploration of the alpha space. The approach distinguishes itself from other LLM-based finance models, which often lack detailed framework descriptions or open-source code, hindering reproducibility.
*   **Significance:** The significance stems from the potential to improve the efficiency and interpretability of alpha factor mining. Traditional methods are often computationally expensive and produce opaque formulas. The proposed framework offers a more structured search process, guided by financial theory and backtesting results, which can lead to better generalization and easier interpretation for practitioners.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   The integration of LLMs and MCTS is a well-motivated and potentially powerful approach.
    *   The use of backtesting feedback to guide the search process is a practical and insightful design choice.
    *   The frequent subtree avoidance mechanism addresses a common problem in automated formula generation.
    *   The experimental results demonstrate promising performance compared to existing methods, indicating its effectiveness.
    *   Improved interpretability of the mined alphas, a significant advantage over other automated approaches.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The framework is dependent on the capabilities of the LLM. Biases or limitations in the LLM could influence the generated alphas.
    *   Despite the reported improvements in interpretability, the generated formulas may still require expert knowledge to fully understand and validate, especially given that LLMs may inherently favour certain formulas generated by them.
    *   The exploration-exploitation trade-off in the MCTS framework is crucial, and the sensitivity analysis of the hyperparameters B (initial budget) and c (exploration weight) is limited. This area warrants more investigation.
    *   The reliance on backtesting raises concerns about overfitting, despite the overfitting risk assessment by LLM. A more detailed investigation into the framework's out-of-sample performance is necessary.
    *   The reported results and claims are limited by the data used (Chinese A-shares market) and the specific choice of evaluation metrics. Broader validation on other markets and with other metrics is needed.
    *   The practical significance of the "high-performing" alphas in real-world trading may depend on factors not considered in the paper, such as transaction costs and market impact.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper presents a novel and well-motivated approach to formulaic alpha mining with demonstrated improvements over existing methods. However, there are limitations related to the LLM's dependence, overfitting concerns, and limited generalizability to other datasets. The reliance on backtesting might also lead to performance that cannot be replicated in a real trading environment, which is not explored in the study.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### What's Inside Your Diffusion Model? A Score-Based Riemannian Metric to Explore the Data Manifold
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.11128v1)
- **Authors**: Simone Azeglio, Arianna Di Bernardo
- **Abstract**: Recent advances in diffusion models have demonstrated their remarkable ability to capture complex image distributions, but the geometric properties of the learned data manifold remain poorly understood. We address this gap by introducing a score-based Riemannian metric that leverages the Stein score function from diffusion models to characterize the intrinsic geometry of the data manifold without requiring explicit parameterization. Our approach defines a metric tensor in the ambient space that stretches distances perpendicular to the manifold while preserving them along tangential directions, effectively creating a geometry where geodesics naturally follow the manifold's contours. We develop efficient algorithms for computing these geodesics and demonstrate their utility for both interpolation between data points and extrapolation beyond the observed data distribution. Through experiments on synthetic data with known geometry, Rotated MNIST, and complex natural images via Stable Diffusion, we show that our score-based geodesics capture meaningful transformations that respect the underlying data distribution. Our method consistently outperforms baseline approaches on perceptual metrics (LPIPS) and distribution-level metrics (FID, KID), producing smoother, more realistic image transitions. These results reveal the implicit geometric structure learned by diffusion models and provide a principled way to navigate the manifold of natural images through the lens of Riemannian geometry.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "What's Inside Your Diffusion Model? A Score-Based Riemannian Metric to Explore the Data Manifold":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel method to explore the geometry of data manifolds learned by diffusion models. It proposes a score-based Riemannian metric derived from the Stein score function, which is directly obtained from the trained diffusion model without explicit manifold parameterization. This metric effectively deforms the ambient pixel space to reflect the underlying data distribution, creating a geometry where geodesics naturally follow the manifold's contours.  The paper presents efficient algorithms for computing geodesics and demonstrating their utility for interpolation and extrapolation tasks. Experiments on synthetic data, Rotated MNIST, and Stable Diffusion validate that score-based geodesics capture meaningful transformations, outperforming baselines in perceptual (LPIPS) and distribution-level metrics (FID, KID).

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of using a score-based Riemannian metric to analyze diffusion model manifolds has substantial novelty. While data-dependent metrics and manifold learning techniques have been explored before, this paper offers a unique approach by directly leveraging the score function of a diffusion model. It avoids explicit parameterization, which is a significant advantage, especially in high-dimensional image spaces.  The specific formulation of the metric tensor is a novel contribution.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical gap in understanding the geometric properties learned by diffusion models. Diffusion models have demonstrated impressive generation capabilities, but the structure of the learned data manifold has remained relatively unexplored. By introducing a principled Riemannian geometry framework, this work opens up new avenues for analyzing and manipulating diffusion model representations. The applications for controlled image manipulation,  semantic editing, and bias detection are all potentially significant.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   Strong theoretical foundation rooted in Riemannian geometry and diffusion models.
    *   Efficient algorithms for geodesic computation suitable for high-dimensional image spaces.
    *   Empirical validation on diverse datasets, including synthetic data (with known geometry), Rotated MNIST, and complex natural images via Stable Diffusion, providing robust evidence for the method's effectiveness.
    *   Quantitative and qualitative evaluations show the benefits of score-based geodesics over conventional methods like linear interpolation and noise diffusion.
    * The manifold-aware extrapolation is a good attempt to create more images related to a specific training set.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Computational Complexity: Despite improvements, geodesic computation still involves iterative optimization, which can be computationally demanding compared to direct methods. The paper acknowledges this limitation.
    *  Extrapolation Validation: There is a lack of proper way to quantitatively evaluate manifold extrapolation.
    * Limited Scope of Validation: The experimentation on Stable Diffusion focuses on unconditional generation with an empty text prompt. Exploring the behavior with more complex text prompts and semantic manipulation tasks would provide deeper insights.
    * Hyperparameter Sensitivity: The method introduces several hyperparameters (λ, diffusion timestep, regularization terms in the energy functional), requiring careful tuning for optimal performance. The paper could benefit from more detailed guidelines for parameter selection.

*   **Potential Impact:** This paper has the potential to significantly impact the field of generative modeling by providing a new tool for analyzing, interpreting, and manipulating the latent spaces of diffusion models. It could lead to improved methods for controlled image generation, semantic editing, and representation learning. Furthermore, it provides a basis for understanding how diffusion models encode geometric structure in the data they are trained on.

*   **Overall Assessment:** The paper presents a sound approach that is well-executed both theoretically and empirically. The described approach is not fully automatic and relies on a large set of hyperparameters.

### One Image is Worth a Thousand Words: A Usability Preservable Text-Image Collaborative Erasing Framework
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.11131v1)
- **Authors**: Feiran Li, Qianqian Xu, Shilong Bao, Zhiyong Yang, Xiaochun Cao, Qingming Huang
- **Abstract**: Concept erasing has recently emerged as an effective paradigm to prevent text-to-image diffusion models from generating visually undesirable or even harmful content. However, current removal methods heavily rely on manually crafted text prompts, making it challenging to achieve a high erasure (efficacy) while minimizing the impact on other benign concepts (usability). In this paper, we attribute the limitations to the inherent gap between the text and image modalities, which makes it hard to transfer the intricately entangled concept knowledge from text prompts to the image generation process. To address this, we propose a novel solution by directly integrating visual supervision into the erasure process, introducing the first text-image Collaborative Concept Erasing (Co-Erasing) framework. Specifically, Co-Erasing describes the concept jointly by text prompts and the corresponding undesirable images induced by the prompts, and then reduces the generating probability of the target concept through negative guidance. This approach effectively bypasses the knowledge gap between text and image, significantly enhancing erasure efficacy. Additionally, we design a text-guided image concept refinement strategy that directs the model to focus on visual features most relevant to the specified text concept, minimizing disruption to other benign concepts. Finally, comprehensive experiments suggest that Co-Erasing outperforms state-of-the-art erasure approaches significantly with a better trade-off between efficacy and usability. Codes are available at https://github.com/Ferry-Li/Co-Erasing.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of concept erasing in text-to-image diffusion models. Current text-based erasing methods struggle to balance erasure efficacy and general usability (maintaining quality for benign prompts) due to the gap between text and image modalities. The paper proposes a Text-Image Collaborative Concept Erasing (Co-Erasing) framework. This framework integrates visual supervision by using images corresponding to the undesirable concept as templates during training.  The core idea involves two encoder branches (text and image) and a text-guided image concept refinement strategy to focus on relevant visual features, minimizing disruption to other concepts. Experiments show Co-Erasing achieves a better trade-off between efficacy and usability compared to state-of-the-art methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The main novelty lies in the *explicit* use of images alongside text for concept erasing.  While existing methods primarily rely on text prompt engineering, this work introduces visual cues to guide the erasure process. The decoupled cross-attention mechanism allows for integration of both modalities without altering the architecture at inference. The text-guided image concept refinement module also adds a valuable component, assisting in a finer extraction of the target feature to be erased and boosting the usability.
*   **Significance:** Concept erasing is important for safety and controlling the outputs of generative models. Improving both the efficacy of erasure *and* usability is crucial for real-world deployment. By addressing the limitations of text-only approaches, this paper provides a step forward. Results and ablations support the efficacy of combining the proposed techniques with existing methods, supporting their adoption as extensions for further research.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Statement:** The paper clearly identifies and articulates the limitations of existing text-based concept erasing methods.
    *   **Well-Motivated Approach:**  The rationale for using images as visual templates to bridge the text-image gap is convincing.
    *   **Comprehensive Experiments:** The paper provides a detailed experimental setup, including comparisons against multiple strong baselines and ablation studies. The quantitative and qualitative results provide solid support for the proposed method. It is further interesting that their method can be extended to different erasing frameworks, which is an important argument that the method is not restricted to the backbones and can serve as building block for further research.
    *   **Code Availability:** Code availability is a big plus.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Reliance on Generated Images:** The reliance on self-generated images has limitations.  The model is limited by its own initial understanding of the concept, which could be flawed or incomplete, which could limit the quality of the erased images.
    *   **Limited Generalization of Refinement:** The text-guided refinement strategy requires manually curated prompts for each specific concept which could hinder its usability.
    *   **Incremental Improvement:** While the results show improvements, the gains over the best existing methods (in certain metrics) are sometimes marginal. The overall qualitative improvement feels more substantial than the quantitative ones.
    *   **Limited failure case analyses**: The failure cases lack details to make it clear what features are retained on which concepts they fail

**Overall Assessment:**

The paper presents a solid and well-executed approach to concept erasing. The use of images as visual templates is a novel and effective way to address the limitations of text-only methods. The experiments are comprehensive and the results support the claims made in the paper. While the gains over the best existing methods are sometimes marginal, the qualitative improvements and the increased usability of the approach are significant. With slight improvements, the paper can make a higher impact.

### Scaling Reasoning can Improve Factuality in Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.11140v1)
- **Authors**: Mike Zhang, Johannes Bjerva, Russa Biswas
- **Abstract**: Recent studies on large language model (LLM) reasoning capabilities have demonstrated promising improvements in model performance by leveraging a lengthy thinking process and additional computational resources during inference, primarily in tasks involving mathematical reasoning (Muennighoff et al., 2025). However, it remains uncertain if longer reasoning chains inherently enhance factual accuracy, particularly beyond mathematical contexts. In this work, we thoroughly examine LLM reasoning within complex open-domain question-answering (QA) scenarios. We initially distill reasoning traces from advanced, large-scale reasoning models (QwQ-32B and DeepSeek-R1-671B), then fine-tune a variety of models ranging from smaller, instruction-tuned variants to larger architectures based on Qwen2.5. To enrich reasoning traces, we introduce factual information from knowledge graphs in the form of paths into our reasoning traces. Our experimental setup includes four baseline approaches and six different instruction-tuned models evaluated across a benchmark of six datasets, encompassing over 22.6K questions. Overall, we carry out 168 experimental runs and analyze approximately 1.7 million reasoning traces. Our findings indicate that, within a single run, smaller reasoning models achieve noticeable improvements in factual accuracy compared to their original instruction-tuned counterparts. Moreover, our analysis demonstrates that adding test-time compute and token budgets factual accuracy consistently improves by 2-8%, further confirming the effectiveness of test-time scaling for enhancing performance and consequently improving reasoning accuracy in open-domain QA tasks. We release all the experimental artifacts for further research.
- **Summary**: The paper "Scaling Reasoning can Improve Factuality in Large Language Models" investigates whether longer reasoning chains, enhanced with knowledge graph (KG) information, improve factual accuracy in large language models (LLMs) for complex open-domain question-answering (QA). The authors distill reasoning traces from advanced models, fine-tune variants of Qwen2.5 architecture using these traces and KG-enhanced traces, and evaluate the models on six QA benchmarks. They find that smaller instruction-tuned models improve factual accuracy with KG-enhanced reasoning traces, while larger models show minimal gains. They also demonstrate that increasing test-time compute boosts factual accuracy. Finally, they find an optimal reasoning length around 2K tokens.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of using knowledge graphs to enhance reasoning traces and improve factual accuracy is not entirely new. However, this paper's comprehensive approach, involving a large-scale empirical study with various LLM sizes, multiple datasets, and detailed analysis of reasoning traces, presents a valuable contribution. The exploration of test-time scaling in conjunction with KG-enhanced reasoning adds further novelty.
*   **Significance:** Improving the factual accuracy of LLMs is a crucial research area. By showing that KG-enhanced reasoning and test-time scaling can lead to tangible improvements, the paper provides practical insights for developing more reliable QA systems. The findings regarding the optimal reasoning length are also valuable for optimizing inference efficiency. The release of the datasets and code contributes to reproducibility and further research in the field.
*   **Strengths:** The main strength is the scope of experimentation (168 runs analyzing 1.7M reasoning traces), which allows for strong claims regarding the behavior of the proposed techniques. The comparison between different Qwen2.5 models (0.5–72B) and the examination of budget forcing and parallel scaling provide a comprehensive picture of the impact of different factors on factual accuracy. The detailed analysis of the results with LLM-as-a-judge and semantic similarity metrics enhances the robustness of the findings. The release of the reasoning artifacts contributes to the open-source scientific community.
*   **Weaknesses:** One weakness is the reliance on LLM-as-a-judge and semantic similarity metrics for evaluating factual accuracy. While these metrics have become more common, they are still not perfect and can introduce biases. A more rigorous approach involving human evaluation of a subset of the answers could further strengthen the paper. While the paper addresses potential data leakage issues to some extent, the analysis could be further strengthened by more direct measurements. Another potential limitation is that the paper focuses on a single family of models (Qwen2.5) and may not generalize to other LLM architectures. The knowledge graph integration, while valuable, is somewhat simplistic, and more sophisticated techniques for leveraging KG information could be explored.
*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to influence future research on improving the factual accuracy of LLMs. The insights into the effectiveness of KG-enhanced reasoning and test-time scaling can guide the development of more reliable QA systems. The finding on the optimal reasoning length has implications for optimizing inference costs. Finally, the release of the artifacts can be used to train more powerful models and to develop better fact verification techniques.

**Overall:** This paper presents a significant contribution to the field of LLM factuality, backed by comprehensive experimentation. While there are certain weaknesses relating to the evaluation methods used and scope of analysis, it's likely to have a solid impact.

### Human-Aligned Bench: Fine-Grained Assessment of Reasoning Ability in MLLMs vs. Humans
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.11141v1)
- **Authors**: Yansheng Qiu, Li Xiao, Zhaopan Xu, Pengfei Zhou, Zheng Wang, Kaipeng Zhang
- **Abstract**: The goal of achieving Artificial General Intelligence (AGI) is to imitate humans and surpass them. Models such as OpenAI's o1, o3, and DeepSeek's R1 have demonstrated that large language models (LLMs) with human-like reasoning capabilities exhibit exceptional performance and are being gradually integrated into multimodal large language models (MLLMs). However, whether these models possess capabilities comparable to humans in handling reasoning tasks remains unclear at present. In this paper, we propose Human-Aligned Bench, a benchmark for fine-grained alignment of multimodal reasoning with human performance. Specifically, we collected 9,794 multimodal questions that solely rely on contextual reasoning, including bilingual (Chinese and English) multimodal questions and pure text-based questions, encompassing four question types: visual reasoning, definition judgment, analogical reasoning, and logical judgment. More importantly, each question is accompanied by human success rates and options that humans are prone to choosing incorrectly. Extensive experiments on the Human-Aligned Bench reveal notable differences between the performance of current MLLMs in multimodal reasoning and human performance. The findings on our benchmark provide insights into the development of the next-generation models.
- **Summary**: Here's a concise summary and a critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "Human-Aligned Bench," a new benchmark for evaluating the fine-grained reasoning abilities of multimodal large language models (MLLMs) against human performance. The benchmark consists of 9,794 questions covering visual reasoning, definition judgment, analogical reasoning, and logical judgment, including bilingual (Chinese and English) and text-only questions. Crucially, the dataset incorporates human accuracy rates and commonly chosen incorrect options, enabling a detailed analysis of how MLLM performance aligns with human cognitive processes. The paper evaluates several open-source and proprietary MLLMs using the benchmark, highlighting performance gaps between MLLMs and humans, especially in visual reasoning and modality robustness. The authors also identify instances of "fake reasoning" in MLLMs, where models rely on memorized patterns rather than genuine reasoning.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper offers a valuable contribution through its Human-Aligned Bench benchmark, which stands out from existing resources in a few key ways:

*   **Fine-grained Human Alignment:** The inclusion of human accuracy rates and error-prone options provides a level of granularity not often seen in existing benchmarks, allowing for a more nuanced comparison between MLLM and human reasoning. This enables researchers to evaluate how human-like the models' reasoning processes are.
*   **Contextual Reasoning Focus:** The benchmark is designed to minimize the influence of world knowledge and focuses solely on reasoning ability, addressing a limitation in many other tasks.
*   **Comprehensive Coverage:** The benchmark balances the coverage of visual and text-based reasoning, as well as different types of reasoning skills, which is a notable improvement over existing visual-centric datasets. Also, the bilingual aspects of the benchmark provides some more diversity in terms of cultural and linguistic awareness than most benchmarks.

**Significance:** The paper's findings have implications for the development and evaluation of future MLLMs.

*   The benchmark provides a useful tool for researchers to analyze and improve the reasoning capabilities of MLLMs, particularly in aligning their performance with human cognitive processes.
*   The study highlights current limitations in MLLMs, especially in areas such as visual reasoning, modality robustness, and "fake reasoning", offering guidance for future research directions.
*   By focusing on contextual reasoning, the work directly addresses a core aspect of achieving Artificial General Intelligence (AGI).

**Weaknesses:** The paper has some weaknesses that should be acknowledged.

*   The benchmark, while focusing on contextual reasoning, does rely heavily on questions drawn from a specific source (Chinese civil service exams). While this provides a consistent style and reduces reliance on world knowledge, it also introduces potential biases. It is crucial to know if these types of questions are culturally biased and if the responses reflect only the reasoning capability.
*   The experiments are limited in scope, focusing on a specific set of models and a specific set of prompt templates. The findings would be strengthened by evaluations across a wider range of models and prompting strategies.
*   The analysis of "fake reasoning" is preliminary and could benefit from a deeper dive into the mechanisms by which models learn and apply reasoning processes.

**Justification for Score:**

While the paper has some weaknesses, the introduction of the Human-Aligned Bench is a significant contribution. The benchmark fills a gap in the existing landscape by providing a fine-grained assessment of reasoning abilities with a focus on human alignment and pure contextualization. The insights gained from the benchmark's use are valuable for the community. Given the novelty of the dataset and the clear direction of future research it provides, the overall impact, I assign a score of 7.

### STEP: A Unified Spiking Transformer Evaluation Platform for Fair and Reproducible Benchmarking
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.11151v1)
- **Authors**: Sicheng Shen, Dongcheng Zhao, Linghao Feng, Zeyang Yue, Jindong Li, Tenglong Li, Guobin Shen, Yi Zeng
- **Abstract**: Spiking Transformers have recently emerged as promising architectures for combining the efficiency of spiking neural networks with the representational power of self-attention. However, the lack of standardized implementations, evaluation pipelines, and consistent design choices has hindered fair comparison and principled analysis. In this paper, we introduce \textbf{STEP}, a unified benchmark framework for Spiking Transformers that supports a wide range of tasks, including classification, segmentation, and detection across static, event-based, and sequential datasets. STEP provides modular support for diverse components such as spiking neurons, input encodings, surrogate gradients, and multiple backends (e.g., SpikingJelly, BrainCog). Using STEP, we reproduce and evaluate several representative models, and conduct systematic ablation studies on attention design, neuron types, encoding schemes, and temporal modeling capabilities. We also propose a unified analytical model for energy estimation, accounting for spike sparsity, bitwidth, and memory access, and show that quantized ANNs may offer comparable or better energy efficiency. Our results suggest that current Spiking Transformers rely heavily on convolutional frontends and lack strong temporal modeling, underscoring the need for spike-native architectural innovations. The full code is available at: https://github.com/Fancyssc/STEP
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "STEP: A Unified Spiking Transformer Evaluation Platform for Fair and Reproducible Benchmarking":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces STEP, a unified benchmark platform for Spiking Transformers (STs). It addresses the lack of standardized evaluation and inconsistent design choices that have hindered fair comparison and analysis of STs. STEP supports various tasks (classification, segmentation, detection) across static, event-based, and sequential datasets. It offers modular support for different components like spiking neurons, input encodings, and backends (SpikingJelly, BrainCog). The authors reproduce and evaluate representative ST models, conduct ablation studies, propose an energy estimation model, and compare STs to quantized ANNs. The results suggest that current STs rely heavily on convolutional frontends and lack strong temporal modeling.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Addressing a Clear Need:** The paper directly tackles a significant issue in the rapidly growing field of Spiking Transformers: the lack of a standardized benchmarking platform. This is crucial for fostering reproducible research and fair comparisons.
    *   **Comprehensive Scope:** STEP provides support for a diverse range of tasks, datasets, and components, making it a valuable tool for researchers exploring different aspects of STs.
    *   **Modular Design:** The modular architecture of STEP allows for easy integration of new components and customization of experiments.
    *   **Energy Efficiency Analysis:** The paper proposes a more refined energy model that includes memory access costs, which is a significant improvement over existing energy estimations.
    *   **Reproducibility:** The emphasis on reproducing existing models and conducting ablation studies enhances the credibility of the benchmark.
    *   **Identification of Current Limitations:** The conclusion that STs are heavily reliant on convolutional frontends and lack strong temporal modeling is a key insight that guides future research.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Novelty in Core Components:** While the framework itself is novel, the individual components (neuron models, encodings, etc.) are mostly based on existing work. The primary contribution is the *integration* and *standardization*, not necessarily the invention of new modules.
    *   **Limited Novelty in the Energy Estimation model:** While it presents memory access cost, that concept isn't novel in the energy-aware hardware community.
    *   **Reproduced results may still suffer from implementation differences.** The authors highlight this point themselves as one of the major challenges during the model reproduction.

*   **Significance:**

    *   **Standardization:** STEP provides a much-needed standardized environment for evaluating STs, promoting reproducibility and facilitating fair comparisons.
    *   **Benchmarking:** STEP helps to accelerate progress in the field.
    *   **Future Research Directions:** The insights gained from the ablation studies and energy analysis can guide future research efforts in developing more efficient and effective ST architectures.

*   **Novelty:** The most novel aspect of the paper is the creation of the integrated framework, STEP, which enables standardized benchmarking for spiking transformers and a deeper understanding of their capabilities and limitations. The proposed energy model, while offering improvements, builds upon existing research in energy estimation and is of less significant novelty. The individual components integrated into the framework are also largely based on previous work. The novelty mainly stems from the systematic organization and analysis enabled by the platform.

**Justification for Score:**

STEP addresses a critical need in the ST field and significantly enhances the ability to compare different architectures under controlled conditions. The energy efficiency insights also offer practical guidelines. While the framework builds upon existing techniques and models, the systematic integration, standardization, and comprehensive analysis makes it a valuable tool for the community.

However, the score is limited by the lack of strong algorithmic novelty in the individual components or energy model. The major breakthrough comes from identifying weaknesses and providing a tool to advance the spiking transformer field in a more meaningful direction.
