### GODBench: A Benchmark for Multimodal Large Language Models in Video Comment Art
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.11436v1)
- **Authors**: Chenkai Zhang, Yiming Lei, Zeming Liu, Haitao Leng, Shaoguo Liu, Tingting Gao, Qingjie Liu, Yunhong Wang
- **Abstract**: Video Comment Art enhances user engagement by providing creative content that conveys humor, satire, or emotional resonance, requiring a nuanced and comprehensive grasp of cultural and contextual subtleties. Although Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) and Chain-of-Thought (CoT) have demonstrated strong reasoning abilities in STEM tasks (e.g. mathematics and coding), they still struggle to generate creative expressions such as resonant jokes and insightful satire. Moreover, existing benchmarks are constrained by their limited modalities and insufficient categories, hindering the exploration of comprehensive creativity in video-based Comment Art creation. To address these limitations, we introduce GODBench, a novel benchmark that integrates video and text modalities to systematically evaluate MLLMs' abilities to compose Comment Art. Furthermore, inspired by the propagation patterns of waves in physics, we propose Ripple of Thought (RoT), a multi-step reasoning framework designed to enhance the creativity of MLLMs. Extensive experiments reveal that existing MLLMs and CoT methods still face significant challenges in understanding and generating creative video comments. In contrast, RoT provides an effective approach to improve creative composing, highlighting its potential to drive meaningful advancements in MLLM-based creativity. GODBench is publicly available at https://github.com/stan-lei/GODBench-ACL2025.
- **Summary**: Here's a concise summary and critical evaluation of the paper "GODBench: A Benchmark for Multimodal Large Language Models in Video Comment Art":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces GODBench, a new multimodal benchmark dataset designed to evaluate the capabilities of Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) in generating creative and insightful "Video Comment Art." Recognizing the limitations of existing benchmarks in terms of modality and category diversity, GODBench leverages a large-scale dataset of videos paired with high-quality, user-generated comments, encompassing a wide range of topics and creative dimensions.  The authors also propose Ripple of Thought (RoT), a multi-step reasoning framework inspired by wave propagation in physics, aimed at improving the creativity of MLLM generated comments. Experiments on various state-of-the-art MLLMs demonstrate the limitations of current models and the potential of RoT to enhance creative composing in this specific domain.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper demonstrates good novelty in several aspects:
    *   **GODBench Dataset:** Creating a substantial, diverse video and comment dataset with human-vetted "GOD-level comments" is a significant contribution. This addresses a gap in existing benchmarks and provides a challenging testbed for MLLMs. The rigorous annotation process, defining and categorizing five distinct dimensions of comment art (Rhetorical Techniques, Divergent Associations, etc.) adds further value.
    *   **Ripple of Thought (RoT):**  The RoT framework presents a novel approach to improving creative thinking in MLLMs. Drawing inspiration from a physical phenomenon (wave propagation) and adapting it into a multi-step reasoning process for creative generation is innovative. This method of explicitly guiding the model through a creative process has potential benefits beyond just video comment art.
    *   **Evaluation Methodology:** The paper's framework for systematically assessing Comment Art across various creative dimensions is thorough, providing a structured way to evaluate the MLLMs' abilities.

*   **Significance:** The paper's findings contribute to the field by:
    *   **Highlighting Limitations:** Exposing the current limitations of MLLMs in generating human-like creative video comments, even with Chain-of-Thought prompting. This underscores the need for further research into enhancing creative reasoning in these models.
    *   **Providing a Solution Path:** Demonstrating RoT's potential to improve creative composing, offering researchers a tangible method for driving advancement in MLLM-based creativity.
    *   **Setting a Standard:** GODBench could serve as a new standard benchmark for this specific application (Video Comment Art), encouraging further development and refined models with a clear measure of success.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Dataset:** The large scale, diversity, and human-annotated quality of GODBench are major strengths.
    *   **Well-Defined Methodology:** The paper clearly defines the creative dimensions used for evaluation and provides a structured methodology for evaluating performance.
    *   **Clear Motivation:** The paper persuasively motivates the need for a more creative form of AI reasoning and points out the deficiencies of existing systems.
    *   **Empirical Validation:**  The extensive experiments and analyses provide evidence supporting the effectiveness of RoT. The quantitative results clearly show RoT's significant performance improvements across various tasks.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Application Specificity:** The application (Video Comment Art) is somewhat niche. While interesting, the broad applicability of RoT outside of this very specific context might require further investigation.
    *   **Computational Cost:**  As the paper acknowledges, running a full evaluation on GODBench demands significant computational resources and large context windows, potentially limiting accessibility for some researchers.
    *   **GPT-4 as Judge:** Relying heavily on GPT-4 for automated evaluation might introduce biases, even if the results are finally validated by human.

**Potential Influence:**

This paper has the potential to significantly influence the field of creative AI and multimodal learning. GODBench could become a widely adopted benchmark, and the RoT framework could inspire new approaches to enhancing creativity in MLLMs for various applications. Furthermore, it brings an important discussion to the wider MLLM community of the need to focus on more subtle and human-like capabilities.

**Score: 8**

**Justification:**

A score of 8 is justified because the paper presents a solid contribution with a novel dataset and a promising reasoning framework. While the application domain is somewhat specialized and there are some limitations related to GPT-4 reliance and resource requirements, the paper's thorough methodology, empirical validation, and potential for driving future research in creative AI make it a significant contribution to the field. GODBench will allow other researchers to build and test models specifically for creative video comment art in a rigorous, standardized way.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### Is Compression Really Linear with Code Intelligence?
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.11441v1)
- **Authors**: Xianzhen Luo, Shijie Xuyang, Tianhao Cheng, Zheng Chu, Houyi Li, ziqi wang, Siming Huang, Qingfu Zhu, Qiufeng Wang, Xiangyu Zhang, Shuigeng Zhou, Wanxiang Che
- **Abstract**: Understanding the relationship between data compression and the capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs) is crucial, especially in specialized domains like code intelligence. Prior work posited a linear relationship between compression and general intelligence. However, it overlooked the multifaceted nature of code that encompasses diverse programming languages and tasks, and struggled with fair evaluation of modern Code LLMs. We address this by evaluating a diverse array of open-source Code LLMs on comprehensive multi-language, multi-task code benchmarks. To address the challenge of efficient and fair evaluation of pre-trained LLMs' code intelligence, we introduce \textit{Format Annealing}, a lightweight, transparent training methodology designed to assess the intrinsic capabilities of these pre-trained models equitably. Compression efficacy, measured as bits-per-character (BPC), is determined using a novel, large-scale, and previously unseen code validation set derived from GitHub. Our empirical results reveal a fundamental logarithmic relationship between measured code intelligence and BPC. This finding refines prior hypotheses of linearity, which we suggest are likely observations of the logarithmic curve's tail under specific, limited conditions. Our work provides a more nuanced understanding of compression's role in developing code intelligence and contributes a robust evaluation framework in the code domain.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the relationship between code compression and code intelligence in Large Language Models (LLMs). It challenges the previously proposed linear relationship, arguing that code's unique characteristics necessitate a dedicated study.  The authors evaluate several open-source Code LLMs across multi-language, multi-task code benchmarks (OmniCodeBench) and introduce "Format Annealing," a technique to elicit intrinsic model capabilities fairly. They measure compression efficacy using a novel, large-scale code validation set from GitHub, finding a logarithmic relationship between code intelligence and Bits-Per-Character (BPC). The paper suggests prior linear observations were likely capturing the tail of the logarithmic curve under limited conditions, providing a more nuanced understanding of compression's role in code intelligence development and a robust evaluation framework.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper provides several novel contributions: (1) challenging the established linear correlation between code compression and intelligence and proposing a logarithmic relationship, (2) the Format Annealing method for fair evaluation of LLMs on code, (3) the OmniCodeBench, and (4) the large-scale code validation set constructed from GitHub. While some individual components might have overlaps with existing techniques (e.g., continual training), the combination and application to this specific problem are novel. The focus on code, a domain different from general language, strengthens this claim. The use of carefully constructed unseen validation sets is a critical improvement over previous works in this area.

*   **Significance:** The work has significant implications. The nuanced logarithmic relationship offers a more accurate model of how compression relates to intelligence in code, potentially guiding the development of more efficient and capable code LLMs. The Format Annealing method and OmniCodeBench offer practical tools for evaluating and improving code LLMs in a fair and comprehensive manner. The GitHub-derived validation set provides a valuable resource for future research. The insights into the limitations of the prior linear model and the importance of multi-lingual, multi-task evaluation are valuable for the community.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Strong empirical evaluation: The paper provides convincing empirical evidence to support its claims, using a diverse set of models and tasks.
    *   Robust methodology: The Format Annealing method and sliding window BPC address key challenges in evaluating code LLMs, ensuring fair comparisons.
    *   Comprehensive evaluation framework: OmniCodeBench and the validation set provide valuable resources for future research in this area.
    *   Clear and well-written: The paper is easy to understand and follows a logical structure.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   Limited exploration of general language LLMs: The paper focuses primarily on code-specific models, which limits the generalizability of its findings. However, this is a reasonable scope given the specialized domain.
    *   Heavy reliance on Python: While multi-lingual, the validation set and benchmark exhibit a Python bias. This is acknowledged in the paper and identified as a direction for future work.
    *   BPC as a Perfect proxy: While BPC is a well-established metric for code compression, it might be not a perfect proxy for "code intelligence" because code intelligence is a very broad concept. While the paper acknowledges this limitation, it could be emphasized more.

*   **Impact:** This work has the potential to significantly influence the direction of research in code LLMs. The findings and methodologies offer a more accurate and robust way to evaluate and develop these models, leading to more efficient and capable tools for software development.

**Justification for Score:**

This is a strong and valuable contribution to the field. The paper challenges a previously held assumption, provides substantial empirical evidence to support a revised understanding, and introduces practical tools for evaluation and development. The weaknesses are acknowledged and represent avenues for future work rather than fundamental flaws. The carefully constructed validation set and evaluation frameworks contribute significantly.

### A Generative Framework for Causal Estimation via Importance-Weighted Diffusion Distillation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.11444v1)
- **Authors**: Xinran Song, Tianyu Chen, Mingyuan Zhou
- **Abstract**: Estimating individualized treatment effects from observational data is a central challenge in causal inference, largely due to covariate imbalance and confounding bias from non-randomized treatment assignment. While inverse probability weighting (IPW) is a well-established solution to this problem, its integration into modern deep learning frameworks remains limited. In this work, we propose Importance-Weighted Diffusion Distillation (IWDD), a novel generative framework that combines the pretraining of diffusion models with importance-weighted score distillation to enable accurate and fast causal estimation-including potential outcome prediction and treatment effect estimation. We demonstrate how IPW can be naturally incorporated into the distillation of pretrained diffusion models, and further introduce a randomization-based adjustment that eliminates the need to compute IPW explicitly-thereby simplifying computation and, more importantly, provably reducing the variance of gradient estimates. Empirical results show that IWDD achieves state-of-the-art out-of-sample prediction performance, with the highest win rates compared to other baselines, significantly improving causal estimation and supporting the development of individualized treatment strategies. We will release our PyTorch code for reproducibility and future research.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "A Generative Framework for Causal Estimation via Importance-Weighted Diffusion Distillation":

**Summary:**

This paper introduces a novel generative framework called Importance-Weighted Diffusion Distillation (IWDD) for causal estimation from observational data. IWDD combines pretraining a conditional diffusion model with importance-weighted score distillation. The core idea is to leverage diffusion models to capture complex data distributions while mitigating confounding bias through importance weighting. Importantly, the authors propose a randomization-based adjustment to the distillation process, eliminating the need for explicit propensity score estimation and provably reducing gradient variance. Empirical results on synthetic and benchmark datasets demonstrate that IWDD achieves state-of-the-art performance in potential outcome prediction and causal effect estimation, outperforming existing methods in out-of-sample generalization and robustness to covariate shift.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a genuinely novel integration of diffusion models and importance weighting within the causal inference framework. The randomization-based adjustment in the distillation process is a particularly innovative contribution. Combining pretraining with distillation is an effective training strategy that allows to adjust for confounds.
*   **Technical Contribution:** The theoretical analysis demonstrating the reduction in gradient variance through the proposed adjustment is a solid technical contribution. The theoretical results that explain the lower gradient variance is appreciated.
*   **Empirical Results:** The empirical evaluation is comprehensive, spanning synthetic data and several established benchmark datasets. The results consistently show IWDD outperforming existing methods, highlighting its potential for real-world applications. It also showed superior performance in the difficult out-of-sample setting.
*   **Practical Impact:**  By creating a more efficient and accurate causal estimation method, IWDD has the potential to facilitate the development of individualized treatment recommendations, particularly in data-scarce scenarios.
*   **Code Availability:** The promise to release PyTorch code promotes reproducibility and further research in this area.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Assumptions:** The framework relies on the standard causal inference assumptions, particularly the unconfoundedness assumption. This limits its applicability in scenarios with unmeasured confounders.
*   **Complexity:**  Diffusion models are inherently complex. While distillation helps, the overall framework requires significant computational resources and expertise for implementation and tuning. The need for pretraining adds further computational overhead.
*   **Limited Scope:** The current work focuses on binary treatments and continuous outcomes. Extending IWDD to more complex settings (e.g., multiple treatments, discrete outcomes, longitudinal data) would enhance its versatility.
*   **Explanation of superior performance**: The paper shows good empirical results, but a deeper analysis as to *why* this approach is superior is lacking. Showing, for example, that the propensity scores are estimated more accurately or that the covariate imbalance is more efficiently accounted for would support the results even more.
*   **Comparison issues**: A comparison to GANITE might not be well suited as it is known that GANs in general tend to be more unstable to train, hence not ideal to benchmark against. GANITE would have been used as a strong baseline, but might have been implemented using a suboptimal setup.

**Significance:**

The paper makes a significant contribution to the intersection of causal inference and generative modeling. It provides a promising approach for addressing confounding bias in observational data, offering potential benefits for various domains, including healthcare and policy-making. The improved out-of-sample generalization is particularly valuable.

**Justification for Score:**

While the paper presents a well-executed and innovative approach, it's crucial to consider the limitations regarding assumptions and complexity. The framework's reliance on unconfoundedness is a significant constraint. Furthermore, while the results are compelling, the real-world impact will depend on its accessibility and usability within diverse application contexts. Therefore, while the paper offers a strong foundation for future research, some issues need to be properly explained and the framework's complexity limits its applicability.

### LLMs unlock new paths to monetizing exploits
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.11449v1)
- **Authors**: Nicholas Carlini, Milad Nasr, Edoardo Debenedetti, Barry Wang, Christopher A. Choquette-Choo, Daphne Ippolito, Florian Tramèr, Matthew Jagielski
- **Abstract**: We argue that Large language models (LLMs) will soon alter the economics of cyberattacks. Instead of attacking the most commonly used software and monetizing exploits by targeting the lowest common denominator among victims, LLMs enable adversaries to launch tailored attacks on a user-by-user basis. On the exploitation front, instead of human attackers manually searching for one difficult-to-identify bug in a product with millions of users, LLMs can find thousands of easy-to-identify bugs in products with thousands of users. And on the monetization front, instead of generic ransomware that always performs the same attack (encrypt all your data and request payment to decrypt), an LLM-driven ransomware attack could tailor the ransom demand based on the particular content of each exploited device. We show that these two attacks (and several others) are imminently practical using state-of-the-art LLMs. For example, we show that without any human intervention, an LLM finds highly sensitive personal information in the Enron email dataset (e.g., an executive having an affair with another employee) that could be used for blackmail. While some of our attacks are still too expensive to scale widely today, the incentives to implement these attacks will only increase as LLMs get cheaper. Thus, we argue that LLMs create a need for new defense-in-depth approaches.
- **Summary**: Here is a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper argues that Large Language Models (LLMs) will fundamentally alter the landscape of cyberattacks, specifically in financially motivated attacks.  It posits that LLMs will enable attackers to move beyond generic, wide-scale attacks (aimed at maximal breadth) and very targeted (but manual, deep) attacks to a regime where attacks can be both broad and deep.  The authors argue that LLMs commodify "intelligence," enabling attackers to adapt and tailor exploits to individual victims and systems, exploiting long-tail systems which are normally not targeted due to economic infeasibility.  The paper presents several case studies with proof-of-concept implementations demonstrating how current LLMs could automate finding sensitive information, exploit vulnerable systems, and mimic trusted entities for phishing. The paper identifies possible new attacks such as a botnet with target specific goals vs generic DDoS. The authors note several potential threats, possible future issues, and potential defenses against these new types of attacks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*Novelty:*

The core idea – that LLMs can fundamentally change the economics of cybercrime by enabling targeted attacks at scale – is reasonably novel. The paper successfully builds on this idea through a good mix of case studies, and the paper effectively integrates security concerns with the use of LLMs. Some of the individual attack vectors presented (e.g., using LLMs to craft phishing emails or guess passwords) have been explored before in a less comprehensive setting, but the systematic exploration of how LLMs can alter the entire attack landscape is novel. Furthermore, the specific focus on "achieving depth and breadth" is well-articulated.

*Significance:*

The significance of the paper is high.  If the authors' arguments hold true, the implications for cybersecurity are substantial.  The shift towards targeted attacks at scale would require a fundamental rethinking of defense strategies. The paper is a strong call-to-action for researchers and practitioners to develop new defense mechanisms specifically designed to counter LLM-driven threats.
The value of the work is high, because a significant shift in thinking in the cybersecurity community is needed to stay ahead of bad actors. The threat is clear.

*Strengths:*

*   **Clear Thesis:** The paper presents a clear and well-defined thesis about the impact of LLMs on cybercrime economics.
*   **Comprehensive Case Studies:** The case studies are well-chosen and provide concrete examples of how LLMs could be used in various attack scenarios. The inclusion of proof-of-concept implementations strengthens the arguments.
*   **Well-reasoned Arguments:** The authors provide a strong rationale for each attack scenario and discuss the potential limitations and challenges.
*   **Thorough Discussion:** The authors touch on potential defenses and future research directions to address the issues they raised.

*Weaknesses:*

*   **Reliance on Future LLM Capabilities:** While the paper demonstrates the *potential* of LLMs, some of the described attacks rely on future improvements in LLM capabilities and cost reduction. It would be more persuasive to base the argument on already existing commercially-available models.
*   **Limited Empirical Validation:** While the proof-of-concept implementations are valuable, they are still relatively simple. The scale of real-world attacks may present additional challenges that are not fully captured in the experiments.
*   **Ethical Concerns not fully explored.** Some of the attacks such as the one using Enron audio could harm real users, so an analysis of how to address this issue is needed in an academic paper.

*Potential Influence:*

The paper has the potential to influence research in several areas:

*   **AI Safety and Security:**  It highlights the importance of considering the security implications of AI technologies.
*   **Cybersecurity:**  It calls for the development of new defense strategies to counter LLM-driven attacks.
*   **Privacy:** It emphasizes the need for better protection of sensitive data in the age of AI.

*Overall Assessment:*

This is an important paper that raises critical questions about the future of cybersecurity in the age of LLMs. While some of the arguments rely on future developments, the paper's core thesis is compelling, and the case studies provide valuable insights into the potential threats.

### ProxyPrompt: Securing System Prompts against Prompt Extraction Attacks
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.11459v1)
- **Authors**: Zhixiong Zhuang, Maria-Irina Nicolae, Hui-Po Wang, Mario Fritz
- **Abstract**: The integration of large language models (LLMs) into a wide range of applications has highlighted the critical role of well-crafted system prompts, which require extensive testing and domain expertise. These prompts enhance task performance but may also encode sensitive information and filtering criteria, posing security risks if exposed. Recent research shows that system prompts are vulnerable to extraction attacks, while existing defenses are either easily bypassed or require constant updates to address new threats. In this work, we introduce ProxyPrompt, a novel defense mechanism that prevents prompt leakage by replacing the original prompt with a proxy. This proxy maintains the original task's utility while obfuscating the extracted prompt, ensuring attackers cannot reproduce the task or access sensitive information. Comprehensive evaluations on 264 LLM and system prompt pairs show that ProxyPrompt protects 94.70% of prompts from extraction attacks, outperforming the next-best defense, which only achieves 42.80%.
- **Summary**: This paper introduces ProxyPrompt, a novel defense mechanism against prompt extraction attacks on large language models (LLMs). The method replaces the original system prompt with a proxy that maintains the task's utility while obfuscating the extracted prompt, preventing attackers from reproducing the task or accessing sensitive information. The authors conduct extensive evaluations on 264 LLM and system prompt pairs, demonstrating that ProxyPrompt protects 94.70% of prompts from extraction attacks, significantly outperforming existing defense strategies. The paper also introduces semantic-level metrics for more precise evaluation of prompt leaks and demonstrates that the optimized proxy prompts can be seamlessly combined with non-sensitive prompts to extend system functionality without compromising security. The paper validates the method’s effectiveness by protecting a popular HuggingChat assistant and longer chain-of-thought (CoT) system prompts.

**Rigorous and Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The idea of replacing the original system prompt with a functionally equivalent but semantically distinct proxy is a relatively novel approach in the area of prompt injection defenses. While other works have explored prompt-based or filtering-based techniques, ProxyPrompt takes a different tack by directly obfuscating the prompt itself. The optimization process in the embedding space to balance utility and obfuscation also adds to the novelty.

**Significance:** Prompt extraction is a serious vulnerability in LLM-based applications, potentially exposing sensitive information and undermining security measures. ProxyPrompt's high success rate in protecting against these attacks, demonstrated across a diverse set of models and tasks, suggests it has significant potential in addressing this problem. The method's ability to maintain utility while ensuring that extracted prompts are unusable is a key strength.  Furthermore, the introduction of more accurate metrics for detecting semantic prompt leaks improves the evaluation of such defenses.

**Strengths:**
*   The paper presents a well-defined approach with a clear motivation and methodology.
*   The extensive experimental evaluation across various LLMs, tasks, and prompt types provides strong evidence of the method's effectiveness.
*   The results showing significant improvements over existing defenses are compelling.
*   The introduction of semantic-level metrics addresses a key limitation of existing evaluation methods.
*   The case study on HuggingChat provides practical validation in a real-world application.
*   The analysis of combining proxy prompts with non-sensitive instructions and the impact of different target prompts demonstrate the adaptability and potential for further refinements of the technique.

**Weaknesses:**
*   The attacker's knowledge is limited. A more powerful attacker model with access to more information about the LLM architecture or the proxy optimization process could potentially compromise the defense. While the paper does use a diverse set of queries, the evaluation could benefit from considering stronger adversarial attacks.
*   The optimization process requires access to the model's weights or an API for prompt optimization. While the paper mentions the possibility of a prompt optimization API for closed-source models, this might be a barrier to adoption for some developers.
*   The reliance on a "representative" dataset of queries for optimization is another potential weakness. The effectiveness of ProxyPrompt could be sensitive to the choice of this dataset, and further work could explore methods for generating or augmenting it.  The paper mentions this, but the extent of impact could be further explored.
*   The choice of specific hyperparameters (learning rate, number of epochs, batch size, etc.) might not be optimal for all LLMs and tasks. A more adaptive or automated hyperparameter tuning process could improve performance.
*   While the paper shows it works on longer CoT prompts, the computational cost associated with optimization for very long prompts is not fully explored.

**Potential Influence on the Field:**
ProxyPrompt has the potential to influence the design and implementation of prompt injection defenses in LLM-based applications.  The approach of obfuscating the prompt itself, combined with the semantic-level evaluation metrics, could inspire further research and development in this area. The paper demonstrates that achieving strong defenses is possible without severely affecting the usability of the LLM applications.
The paper acknowledges a limited attacker knowledge, and indicates that the results are a lower bound on the method, implying further work is desired.

**Justification for Score:**
While ProxyPrompt presents a novel and effective defense mechanism, some limitations need to be addressed. The experimental evaluation is thorough, but a broader evaluation of different attack scenarios could improve the analysis. The paper is very good, but there are identified areas of future work and limitations that restrain its novelty and significance.

### Disentangling Reasoning and Knowledge in Medical Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.11462v1)
- **Authors**: Rahul Thapa, Qingyang Wu, Kevin Wu, Harrison Zhang, Angela Zhang, Eric Wu, Haotian Ye, Suhana Bedi, Nevin Aresh, Joseph Boen, Shriya Reddy, Ben Athiwaratkun, Shuaiwen Leon Song, James Zou
- **Abstract**: Medical reasoning in large language models (LLMs) aims to emulate clinicians' diagnostic thinking, but current benchmarks such as MedQA-USMLE, MedMCQA, and PubMedQA often mix reasoning with factual recall. We address this by separating 11 biomedical QA benchmarks into reasoning- and knowledge-focused subsets using a PubMedBERT classifier that reaches 81 percent accuracy, comparable to human performance. Our analysis shows that only 32.8 percent of questions require complex reasoning. We evaluate biomedical models (HuatuoGPT-o1, MedReason, m1) and general-domain models (DeepSeek-R1, o4-mini, Qwen3), finding consistent gaps between knowledge and reasoning performance. For example, m1 scores 60.5 on knowledge but only 47.1 on reasoning. In adversarial tests where models are misled with incorrect initial reasoning, biomedical models degrade sharply, while larger or RL-trained general models show more robustness. To address this, we train BioMed-R1 using fine-tuning and reinforcement learning on reasoning-heavy examples. It achieves the strongest performance among similarly sized models. Further gains may come from incorporating clinical case reports and training with adversarial and backtracking scenarios.
- **Summary**: This paper tackles a crucial problem in the evaluation of medical reasoning capabilities in large language models (LLMs): the conflation of knowledge retrieval with genuine reasoning.  The authors address this by disentangling knowledge-heavy and reasoning-heavy questions across a comprehensive set of 11 biomedical QA benchmarks. They achieve this via a fine-tuned PubMedBERT classifier that demonstrates human-level performance in distinguishing question types.  Their analysis reveals that only a minority (32.8%) of benchmark questions necessitate complex reasoning, with the bulk focused on factual recall.

Using this refined dataset, the authors evaluate several recent biomedical reasoning models and observe a consistent performance gap between knowledge and reasoning tasks.  Furthermore, they perform adversarial evaluations by pre-filling models with incorrect answers, revealing significant degradation in biomedical models' performance, while general-domain models show more resilience. The paper concludes by training BioMed-R1-8B, using supervised fine-tuning and reinforcement learning on reasoning-heavy examples, and demonstrates improved overall and adversarial performance compared to similar-sized models. They suggest incorporating additional reasoning-rich data and adversarial/backtracking training scenarios to further enhance model robustness and reliability.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Addressing a Significant Problem:** The paper directly tackles a fundamental issue in medical LLM evaluation, which is the reliance on benchmarks that do not accurately assess reasoning abilities.
    *   **Rigorous Methodology:** The use of a PubMedBERT classifier with human-level performance for question classification provides a strong basis for disentangling question types. The adversarial evaluation setup is also well-designed and provides valuable insights into model robustness.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:**  The evaluation spans a broad range of biomedical QA benchmarks and includes recent, relevant models.
    *   **Actionable Insights:** The paper provides specific suggestions for future research directions, including the use of reasoning-rich data sources and adversarial/backtracking training techniques.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Classifier Imperfection:** While the PubMedBERT classifier achieves high accuracy, the inherent subjectivity of distinguishing knowledge and reasoning means there will inevitably be misclassifications. This can affect the reliability of the downstream analyses.
    *   **Limited Model Scope:** The evaluation primarily focuses on Llama-based models and a few other biomedical models, potentially missing insights from other architectural choices or fine-tuning strategies.
    *   **Incremental Improvement:** The BioMed-R1-8B model shows a modest improvement in reasoning accuracy, indicating the difficulty of the task and suggesting that more substantial innovations are needed.
    *   **GRPO hyperparameter tuning details in Supp Mat only:** RL performance is highly sensitive to hyperparameter tuning, so there should be more thorough discussion and justification of design choices within the main text.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**
    *   The **disentanglement of reasoning and knowledge** in existing medical QA benchmarks is a novel and significant contribution. This provides a more accurate assessment of model capabilities and can guide future model development efforts.
    *   The **adversarial evaluation methodology** is a valuable approach for assessing model robustness and reliability, and its application to the medical domain is particularly important for safety considerations.
    *   The **training of BioMed-R1-8B** using reasoning-heavy examples provides empirical evidence that targeted training can improve reasoning performance. However, the gains are not dramatic, indicating that more research is needed in this area.

Overall, the paper represents a valuable contribution to the field of medical LLMs. It highlights a critical flaw in current evaluation practices, provides a rigorous methodology for disentangling question types, and offers concrete suggestions for improving model reasoning abilities. While the gains achieved with BioMed-R1-8B are incremental, the paper's insights and methodologies have the potential to significantly influence future research directions. The significance rests in shifting the community's focus toward more accurate assessments and targeted improvements in medical reasoning.

### Exploiting Radiance Fields for Grasp Generation on Novel Synthetic Views
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.11467v1)
- **Authors**: Abhishek Kashyap, Henrik Andreasson, Todor Stoyanov
- **Abstract**: Vision based robot manipulation uses cameras to capture one or more images of a scene containing the objects to be manipulated. Taking multiple images can help if any object is occluded from one viewpoint but more visible from another viewpoint. However, the camera has to be moved to a sequence of suitable positions for capturing multiple images, which requires time and may not always be possible, due to reachability constraints. So while additional images can produce more accurate grasp poses due to the extra information available, the time-cost goes up with the number of additional views sampled. Scene representations like Gaussian Splatting are capable of rendering accurate photorealistic virtual images from user-specified novel viewpoints. In this work, we show initial results which indicate that novel view synthesis can provide additional context in generating grasp poses. Our experiments on the Graspnet-1billion dataset show that novel views contributed force-closure grasps in addition to the force-closure grasps obtained from sparsely sampled real views while also improving grasp coverage. In the future we hope this work can be extended to improve grasp extraction from radiance fields constructed with a single input image, using for example diffusion models or generalizable radiance fields.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper explores the use of novel view synthesis, specifically using Gaussian Splatting, to improve grasp generation in robot manipulation. The central hypothesis is that generating synthetic views of a scene using a radiance field can provide additional context for grasp planners, leading to more force-closure grasps and improved grasp coverage (i.e., grasps for more objects in the scene).  The authors use the Graspnet-1billion dataset, construct radiance fields from a small number of real views, generate novel views, and then run a pre-trained grasp detection network on both the real and synthetic views.  The results show that the novel views contribute additional force-closure grasps beyond those found in the real views alone, and that grasp coverage also increases. The paper also explores the effect of different post-processing techniques like pose-NMS and clustering to refine the grasps.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Strengths:**

    * **Clear Problem Definition:**  The paper addresses a practical limitation in robot manipulation – the time and constraints involved in capturing multiple real views for scene understanding. Using novel view synthesis to augment the available data is a relevant approach.
    * **Rigorous Methodology:**  The paper uses a well-established dataset (Graspnet-1billion) and a state-of-the-art radiance field technique (Gaussian Splatting).  The experimental setup is clearly described, and the evaluation metrics (force-closure and grasp coverage) are appropriate.  The inclusion of different post-processing methods demonstrates an effort to refine the results.
    * **Positive Results:** The results support the hypothesis. Novel views do indeed provide additional context, leading to more grasps and better coverage. The histograms clearly illustrate this improvement.
    * **Well-Organized and Written:**  The paper is structured logically and the writing is clear and concise.

* **Weaknesses:**

    * **Limited Novelty:** While the application of Gaussian Splatting to improve grasp generation is sensible, it lacks significant novelty. Applying neural radiance fields to downstream robotic tasks is not new (as acknowledged in the background section). The primary contribution is demonstrating the effectiveness of Gaussian Splatting for *this specific* application.
    * **Reliance on a Pre-trained Network:** The grasp detection network is pre-trained on Graspnet-1billion. This limits the scope of the work because it's unclear how well it generalizes to different environments. The paper doesn't address potential biases or limitations of the pre-trained model.
    * **Simulated Experimentation:** The evaluation is entirely simulation-based. The transferability of these grasps to a real robot is not demonstrated. Real-world issues like sensor noise, calibration errors, and model inaccuracies are not accounted for. The paper acknowledges that "a high number of force-closure grasps does not come with the guarantee that all of them can be executed on the real robot", but doesn't take any steps towards addressing the gap.
    * **Limited Exploration of Viewpoint Selection:**  The paper states that the novel viewpoints were chosen to be close to the real views, expecting better performance from the pre-trained network. This seems a bit limiting. A more thorough investigation into how to select the *best* novel viewpoints would have been more valuable.
    * **Incremental Contribution:** While the paper shows a positive impact, the extent of the improvement doesn't seem transformative. The number of additional grasps, while present, seems more incremental than revolutionary.

* **Significance:**

    * The significance is moderate.  It reinforces the idea that radiance fields can be useful for downstream robotic tasks and demonstrates the utility of Gaussian Splatting in this context. The results may be of interest to researchers working on grasp planning and scene understanding for manipulation. However, the lack of real-world validation and the limited novelty diminish the overall impact.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper presents a sound methodology and interesting, positive results. However, the lack of real-world validation, reliance on a pre-trained network, and moderate novelty limit its overall impact. It essentially applies a recent rendering technique (Gaussian Splatting) to a standard problem (grasp planning). While the application is logical and provides some improvement, it doesn't represent a significant breakthrough or offer substantial new insights into the problem of grasp planning.

### PSDiffusion: Harmonized Multi-Layer Image Generation via Layout and Appearance Alignment
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.11468v1)
- **Authors**: Dingbang Huang, Wenbo Li, Yifei Zhao, Xinyu Pan, Yanhong Zeng, Bo Dai
- **Abstract**: Diffusion models have made remarkable advancements in generating high-quality images from textual descriptions. Recent works like LayerDiffuse have extended the previous single-layer, unified image generation paradigm to transparent image layer generation. However, existing multi-layer generation methods fail to handle the interactions among multiple layers such as rational global layout, physics-plausible contacts and visual effects like shadows and reflections while maintaining high alpha quality. To solve this problem, we propose PSDiffusion, a unified diffusion framework for simultaneous multi-layer text-to-image generation. Our model can automatically generate multi-layer images with one RGB background and multiple RGBA foregrounds through a single feed-forward process. Unlike existing methods that combine multiple tools for post-decomposition or generate layers sequentially and separately, our method introduces a global-layer interactive mechanism that generates layered-images concurrently and collaboratively, ensuring not only high quality and completeness for each layer, but also spatial and visual interactions among layers for global coherence.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "PSDiffusion: Harmonized Multi-Layer Image Generation via Layout and Appearance Alignment" introduces a new diffusion-based framework called PSDiffusion for generating multi-layer images from text prompts.  It addresses limitations in existing multi-layer image generation methods, which often struggle with maintaining consistent layouts, plausible inter-layer interactions (like shadows), and high-quality alpha channels. PSDiffusion introduces a "global-layer interactive mechanism" that includes a layer cross-attention reweighting module for layout coherence and a partial joint self-attention module for inter-layer context modeling. The method fine-tunes Stable Diffusion XL with LoRA.  Additionally, the authors created a new high-quality multi-layer RGBA image dataset called Inter-Layer with artist-grade alpha mattes and realistic layer interactions. The paper presents quantitative and qualitative comparisons against existing methods like LayerDiffuse and ART, along with a user study, demonstrating improved performance in layout harmony, interaction plausibility, and overall image quality.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper demonstrates a good amount of novelty. The key contributions are: (1) The "global-layer interactive mechanism" (cross-attention reweighting and partial joint self-attention) which effectively addresses the limitations in existing multi-layer image generation concerning layout coherence and inter-layer interactions. (2) The creation of the Inter-Layer dataset, which helps to mitigate the lack of high-quality multi-layer RGBA data. Existing approaches depend on methods that are inherently inaccurate for constructing suitable ground truth data. (3) the training and use of an RGBA preserving VAE.

*   **Significance:** The paper has the potential to be significantly impactful in the field of image generation and editing. Multi-layer image representations are crucial for editing flexibility and component sharing in graphics workflows. Generating these representations directly from text opens up new possibilities for content creation and manipulation. The released Inter-Layer dataset is also a valuable contribution that is missing from existing art pipelines.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Strong technical contribution:**  The proposed PSDiffusion framework is well-motivated and technically sound. The integration of cross-attention reweighting and partial joint self-attention is an effective way to leverage layout priors and ensure inter-layer consistency.
    *   **High-quality dataset:** The Inter-Layer dataset is a significant resource for training and evaluating multi-layer image generation models.
    *   **Comprehensive evaluation:** The paper provides a thorough evaluation with quantitative metrics, qualitative comparisons, and a user study, demonstrating the superiority of PSDiffusion over existing methods.
    *   **Clear and well-written:** The paper is easy to follow and understand, with clear explanations of the proposed method and experimental setup.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited generalization assessment:** The paper could benefit from exploring the limitations of the dataset and the generalizability of the fine-tuned model for various prompt compositions.
    *   **Heavy reliance on LLM:** The paper could investigate how the dataset and model performs without reliance on pre-trained models.

*   **Potential Influence:** This work can influence several areas:

    *   **Image editing:** Provides a more intuitive and efficient way to edit images by manipulating individual layers.
    *   **Content creation:**  Facilitates the creation of complex and visually appealing images from textual descriptions.
    *   **Computer graphics:** Advances research on layered image representations and their applications.
    *   **Multi-modal research:** Provides an example of incorporating external tools to perform tasks that were once manual.

**Score:** 8

**Rationale:** The paper makes a significant contribution to the field of multi-layer image generation with a novel and effective framework.  The Inter-Layer dataset is also a valuable resource. However, it may be limited in scalability and generalizability. The model and dataset are promising additions to image generation.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### HelpSteer3-Preference: Open Human-Annotated Preference Data across Diverse Tasks and Languages
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.11475v1)
- **Authors**: Zhilin Wang, Jiaqi Zeng, Olivier Delalleau, Hoo-Chang Shin, Felipe Soares, Alexander Bukharin, Ellie Evans, Yi Dong, Oleksii Kuchaiev
- **Abstract**: Preference datasets are essential for training general-domain, instruction-following language models with Reinforcement Learning from Human Feedback (RLHF). Each subsequent data release raises expectations for future data collection, meaning there is a constant need to advance the quality and diversity of openly available preference data. To address this need, we introduce HelpSteer3-Preference, a permissively licensed (CC-BY-4.0), high-quality, human-annotated preference dataset comprising of over 40,000 samples. These samples span diverse real-world applications of large language models (LLMs), including tasks relating to STEM, coding and multilingual scenarios. Using HelpSteer3-Preference, we train Reward Models (RMs) that achieve top performance on RM-Bench (82.4%) and JudgeBench (73.7%). This represents a substantial improvement (~10% absolute) over the previously best-reported results from existing RMs. We demonstrate HelpSteer3-Preference can also be applied to train Generative RMs and how policy models can be aligned with RLHF using our RMs. Dataset (CC-BY-4.0): https://huggingface.co/datasets/nvidia/HelpSteer3#preference
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "HelpSteer3-Preference: Open Human-Annotated Preference Data across Diverse Tasks and Languages":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces HelpSteer3-Preference, a new, permissively licensed, high-quality, human-annotated preference dataset for training instruction-following language models using Reinforcement Learning from Human Feedback (RLHF). The dataset contains over 40,000 samples covering diverse tasks, including STEM, coding, and multilingual scenarios. The authors demonstrate that reward models (RMs) trained on this dataset achieve state-of-the-art performance on RM-Bench and JudgeBench, significantly improving upon existing RMs. They also show how the data can be used to train Generative RMs and align policy models with RLHF. The dataset is publicly released under a CC-BY-4.0 license.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty of this paper lies in the *scale, diversity, and quality control* applied to the preference data. While existing datasets exist for RLHF, HelpSteer3-Preference expands the coverage to STEM, coding, and multilingual scenarios with specialist annotators. Further, it uses stringent annotation practices to ensure high quality. The use of these diverse tasks in a single dataset is a valuable contribution.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance stems from the improved performance demonstrated by reward models trained on the new dataset. Achieving state-of-the-art results on RM-Bench and JudgeBench, popular benchmarks, suggests that HelpSteer3-Preference facilitates the training of more effective RMs. This is crucial for improving the alignment of LLMs with human preferences across diverse tasks. The permissive licensing makes this dataset accessible to a broader research community.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Diversity:** The inclusion of STEM, coding, and multilingual tasks significantly broadens the scope of preference data, addressing a limitation in previous datasets.
    *   **High-Quality Annotations:** The use of specialist annotators and stringent annotation practices enhance the quality of the data, leading to improved RM performance.
    *   **Permissive License:** The CC-BY-4.0 license promotes accessibility and wider adoption within the research community and commercial contexts.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The paper provides comprehensive experimental results demonstrating the effectiveness of the dataset in training RMs and aligning policy models.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Multilingual Diversity:** Although the dataset includes multilingual samples, it's limited to only 13 natural languages. A more comprehensive representation of languages would further enhance the dataset's utility. The number of samples in the multilingual dataset is also smaller compared to other categories.
    *   **Specific Tasks Only:** Despite the inclusion of STEM and Coding tasks in addition to general tasks, the dataset is still not exhaustive.
    *   **Prompt Quality:** While efforts were made to filter harmful prompts, there's still a potential risk of biases present within the dataset.
    *   **Generalization:** The gains might be specific to the Llama-3.3-70B-Instruct model, and it remains to be seen if similar improvements can be achieved when the same methodology is applied to other state-of-the-art open-weights models.
    *   **Code Subset Limitations:** The authors themselves note that focusing on code solution correctness might overlook style and commenting concerns that were considered in annotations.

*   **Potential Influence:** The release of HelpSteer3-Preference is likely to have a positive influence on the field by providing a valuable resource for training more aligned and capable language models. It will also likely inspire further research into creating more diverse and high-quality preference datasets.

**Justification:**

The paper contributes to addressing the critical need for improved alignment in LLMs. The diverse and high-quality nature of the dataset fills a gap in existing resources. The significant gains demonstrated on standard benchmarks are compelling. However, limitations in multilingual diversity, potential biases in the dataset, and questions regarding generalization prevent the paper from receiving a higher score.

**Score: 7.5**

### Improving Assembly Code Performance with Large Language Models via Reinforcement Learning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.11480v1)
- **Authors**: Anjiang Wei, Tarun Suresh, Huanmi Tan, Yinglun Xu, Gagandeep Singh, Ke Wang, Alex Aiken
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) have demonstrated strong performance across a wide range of programming tasks, yet their potential for code optimization remains underexplored. This work investigates whether LLMs can optimize the performance of assembly code, where fine-grained control over execution enables improvements that are difficult to express in high-level languages. We present a reinforcement learning framework that trains LLMs using Proximal Policy Optimization (PPO), guided by a reward function that considers both functional correctness, validated through test cases, and execution performance relative to the industry-standard compiler gcc -O3. To support this study, we introduce a benchmark of 8,072 real-world programs. Our model, Qwen2.5-Coder-7B-PPO, achieves 96.0% test pass rates and an average speedup of 1.47x over the gcc -O3 baseline, outperforming all 20 other models evaluated, including Claude-3.7-sonnet. These results indicate that reinforcement learning can unlock the potential of LLMs to serve as effective optimizers for assembly code performance.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Improving Assembly Code Performance with Large Language Models via Reinforcement Learning":

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the potential of Large Language Models (LLMs) to optimize assembly code performance. The authors develop a reinforcement learning framework using Proximal Policy Optimization (PPO) to train LLMs. The reward function considers both functional correctness (validated through test cases) and execution speed relative to the `gcc -O3` baseline. They create a new benchmark of 8,072 real-world C programs along with their assembly equivalents generated by `gcc -O3`. Their best model, Qwen2.5-Coder-7B-PPO, achieves a 96.0% test pass rate and a 1.47x average speedup over the `gcc -O3` baseline, outperforming a wide range of other models, including Claude-3.7-sonnet. Ablation studies demonstrate the importance of the reward function design.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

*   **Positive:** The application of LLMs to assembly code optimization, particularly to improve performance beyond what state-of-the-art compilers achieve (i.e., `gcc -O3`), is relatively novel.  Existing work on LLMs for code optimization has primarily focused on higher-level languages like Python and C++. The idea of using reinforcement learning to guide an LLM towards optimizing assembly code by directly manipulating registers and instructions is a fresh approach.
*   **Neutral:** The use of PPO for reinforcement learning is not itself novel, as it's a standard algorithm. The adaptation of PPO to this specific domain is well-executed but relies on existing methodologies.
*   **Negative:**  The idea of using LLMs as code optimizers has been previously explored. This paper is an incremental improvement over existing approaches, focusing specifically on assembly code.

**Significance:**

*   **Positive:**  If the results are robust and generalizable, the work has the potential to impact how compilers and performance-critical code are developed. Outperforming the highly optimized `gcc -O3` baseline is a significant achievement. Demonstrating the capability of LLMs to reason about low-level instruction sequences and perform optimizations that compilers miss is of practical importance.
*   **Neutral:**  The dataset construction is a strong point, providing a valuable resource for the community. However, the selection process – focusing on programs with significant speedup from `-O0` to `-O3` – potentially biases the dataset towards programs that are already amenable to optimization, perhaps making it easier to further optimize them. The significance would be higher if more diverse types of programs were benchmarked.
*   **Negative:** The reliance on test-case validation for correctness is a limitation. It doesn't provide formal guarantees of equivalence, which is a concern for safety-critical applications. The generalizability of the results to different architectures and optimization targets (e.g., code size, energy consumption) is also unclear. The potential for overfitting to the specific hardware used for training and evaluation is a relevant concern.

**Strengths:**

*   A well-defined problem with a practical motivation.
*   A robust and reproducible reinforcement learning framework.
*   A new assembly code optimization benchmark.
*   Demonstration of LLM achieving performance improvements beyond a strong compiler baseline (`gcc -O3`).
*   Thorough experimental evaluation including ablation studies.
*   Case study illustrates the LLM's ability to leverage specialized hardware instructions missed by traditional compilers.

**Weaknesses:**

*   Correctness verification relies solely on test cases, lacking formal guarantees.
*   Potential for overfitting to the training hardware and benchmark.
*   Limited generalizability across architectures and optimization targets.
*   The dataset construction process could introduce bias toward program types.
*   Incremental improvement over existing LLM-based code optimization techniques.

**Potential Influence:**

The paper could inspire further research into:

*   Formal verification techniques for LLM-optimized assembly code.
*   Developing LLMs that can directly compile high-level code to efficient assembly without relying on compiler output as input.
*   Extending the approach to other architectures and optimization goals.
*   Investigating methods to improve the generalizability of LLM optimizers.
*   Using LLMs to guide compiler optimization phases or rewrite rules.

**Rigorous Rationale:**

While the work builds upon prior art in LLM-based code optimization, its focus on assembly code presents unique challenges and opportunities.  The demonstrated ability to surpass `gcc -O3` is noteworthy, suggesting that LLMs can potentially discover optimizations that traditional compilers miss. However, the reliance on test-case validation, and potential for overfitting limits the broader impact. Overall, the paper makes a significant contribution to a niche but important area, and has the potential to spark further innovation in compiler design and code optimization.

**Score: 7**

The paper's novelty is above average due to the relatively unexplored area of assembly code optimization, and its impact can be potentially high (if the test case limitation can be addressed). The significance would be even higher with stronger guarantees of correctness and better generalizability, warranting a slightly higher score.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Unsupervised Detection of Distribution Shift in Inverse Problems using Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.11482v1)
- **Authors**: Shirin Shoushtari, Edward P. Chandler, Yuanhao Wang, M. Salman Asif, Ulugbek S. Kamilov
- **Abstract**: Diffusion models are widely used as priors in imaging inverse problems. However, their performance often degrades under distribution shifts between the training and test-time images. Existing methods for identifying and quantifying distribution shifts typically require access to clean test images, which are almost never available while solving inverse problems (at test time). We propose a fully unsupervised metric for estimating distribution shifts using only indirect (corrupted) measurements and score functions from diffusion models trained on different datasets. We theoretically show that this metric estimates the KL divergence between the training and test image distributions. Empirically, we show that our score-based metric, using only corrupted measurements, closely approximates the KL divergence computed from clean images. Motivated by this result, we show that aligning the out-of-distribution score with the in-distribution score -- using only corrupted measurements -- reduces the KL divergence and leads to improved reconstruction quality across multiple inverse problems.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of detecting distribution shifts in imaging inverse problems when only corrupted measurements are available, a common scenario in real-world applications. It proposes an unsupervised metric, derived from diffusion models, that estimates the KL divergence between the training and test image distributions using only these corrupted measurements. The authors theoretically prove that the metric estimates the true KL divergence under certain assumptions. They empirically demonstrate the metric's effectiveness in approximating the image-domain KL divergence in inpainting and MRI reconstruction tasks.  Finally, they show that aligning out-of-distribution scores with in-distribution scores, using only corrupted measurements, reduces the estimated KL divergence and improves reconstruction quality across these inverse problems.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty (Score: 7):** The paper offers a significant contribution by presenting the first *unsupervised* metric for quantifying distribution shifts in inverse problems directly from corrupted measurements. This is a critical advancement because existing methods generally require clean test images, which are often unattainable in real-world inverse problems. The derivation of a closed-form estimate and its theoretical justification adds to the novelty. However, the novelty is somewhat tempered by the reliance on diffusion models, which themselves are based on existing principles of score-based generative modeling. While the application to OOD detection from corrupted measurements *using diffusion models* is new, the core diffusion modeling technology isn't a breakthrough on its own.

*   **Significance (Score: 8):** The work has substantial significance because it directly addresses a practical limitation of applying diffusion models in imaging inverse problems where distribution shifts and measurement corruptions are commonplace. The ability to detect these shifts without clean data enables more robust and reliable use of diffusion priors. The demonstration of improved reconstruction through a simple adaptation technique that is guided by the proposed KL divergence estimate further enhances the significance. The implication that one can now more reliably deploy diffusion models without needing to retrain on target data, and knowing whether a model might be going OOD based on the measured signal alone makes this a potentially impactful result. The ability to also use this OOD detection metric to guide model adaptation is also highly impactful.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Theoretical Justification:**  The paper provides a rigorous theoretical framework, including proofs that connect the measurement-domain metric to the true KL divergence. This provides a strong foundation for the empirical results.
    *   **Practical Relevance:** The problem addressed is highly relevant, and the proposed solution is directly applicable to real-world inverse problems where clean test images are unavailable.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The experiments on inpainting and MRI reconstruction convincingly demonstrate the metric's accuracy and the benefits of adaptation based on the metric.
    *   **Clear Presentation:** The paper is generally well-written and presents the concepts and results clearly.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Assumptions:** The theoretical results rely on specific assumptions (e.g., shared right singular matrix). While these assumptions hold in certain scenarios like inpainting and undersampled Fourier imaging, they may limit the applicability of the method in other situations. The paper does not extensively analyze the sensitivity of the metric to violations of these assumptions.
    *   **Computational Cost:** While the adaptation method is lightweight, computing the KL divergence metric itself may still be computationally demanding, especially for high-dimensional data and large-scale diffusion models. This aspect isn't thoroughly explored in the paper.
    *   **Limited Generalization Study:** The adaptation experiments are primarily focused on a single OOD scenario (AFHQ). The effectiveness of the proposed adaptation technique in handling more complex or diverse distribution shifts is not extensively investigated.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field by:

    *   Enabling more robust and reliable use of diffusion models in imaging inverse problems.
    *   Inspiring new research on unsupervised OOD detection and adaptation techniques in measurement-limited settings.
    *   Providing a theoretical framework for understanding the relationship between distribution shifts and score function discrepancies in inverse problems.

**Rigorous Rationale for Score:**

The paper makes a valuable contribution to the field of imaging inverse problems. The work is not a groundbreaking theoretical departure, but the shift from image-domain-only reasoning to measurement-domain inference makes this an impactful result. It is a practical, well-supported, and potentially influential contribution. The theoretical work is sound, the derivations are insightful, the evaluation is comprehensive, and the connection to practical diffusion models is clear.
The limitations regarding assumptions and computational cost warrant a slight reduction in the score. A full score would only be appropriate if there was a broader theoretical proof relaxing the strong assumptions of shared operators and full-rank measurement operators, for example.

### msf-CNN: Patch-based Multi-Stage Fusion with Convolutional Neural Networks for TinyML
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.11483v1)
- **Authors**: Zhaolan Huang, Emmanuel Baccelli
- **Abstract**: AI spans from large language models to tiny models running on microcontrollers (MCUs). Extremely memory-efficient model architectures are decisive to fit within an MCU's tiny memory budget e.g., 128kB of RAM. However, inference latency must remain small to fit real-time constraints. An approach to tackle this is patch-based fusion, which aims to optimize data flows across neural network layers. In this paper, we introduce msf-CNN, a novel technique that efficiently finds optimal fusion settings for convolutional neural networks (CNNs) by walking through the fusion solution space represented as a directed acyclic graph. Compared to previous work on CNN fusion for MCUs, msf-CNN identifies a wider set of solutions. We published an implementation of msf-CNN running on various microcontrollers (ARM Cortex-M, RISC-V, ESP32). We show that msf-CNN can achieve inference using 50% less RAM compared to the prior art (MCUNetV2 and StreamNet). We thus demonstrate how msf-CNN offers additional flexibility for system designers.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary and critical evaluation of the paper based on the provided text.

**Summary**

The paper introduces "msf-CNN," a novel technique for optimizing convolutional neural networks (CNNs) to run on resource-constrained microcontrollers (MCUs).  The core idea is patch-based multi-stage fusion, which aims to find optimal fusion settings for CNN layers to minimize memory usage and maintain acceptable inference latency. The authors frame the problem as finding a shortest path in a directed acyclic graph (DAG) representing the CNN architecture.  They present a pruning strategy to reduce the search space and improve efficiency.  They also introduce optimized global pooling and dense layers to further reduce RAM usage.  The paper includes preliminary experimental results on various MCUs, comparing msf-CNN to existing techniques like MCUNetV2 and StreamNet, demonstrating its ability to reduce RAM usage.

**Critical Evaluation**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a system for CNN optimization via patch-based fusion, which is not entirely novel as the core concept has been explored in MCUNetV2 and StreamNet. The key contribution lies in introducing a more systematic approach to finding optimal fusion settings using a graph-based representation and pruning strategies. This allows the algorithm to explore a wider range of solutions than prior heuristic-based approaches like only fusing the beginning layers of a network. The optimizations applied to the global pooling and dense layers do contribute to some amount of originality.

*   **Significance:** The problem addressed is highly significant. Deploying AI on MCUs is crucial for edge computing and IoT applications, but the limited resources pose a major challenge. Reducing memory footprint while maintaining acceptable performance is a key goal. The paper presents promising results, demonstrating a significant reduction in RAM usage compared to existing techniques.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Systematic Approach:** The graph-based formulation and pruning strategy provide a more principled way to find optimal fusion settings compared to prior heuristic methods.
    *   **RAM Reduction:** The experimental results show a substantial reduction in RAM usage, which is crucial for MCUs.
    *   **Flexibility:** The approach allows for trade-offs between memory usage and compute latency, which is valuable for different application requirements.
    *   **Hardware Diversity:** Evaluation on different MCU architectures demonstrates versatility.
    *   **Inclusion of Open Source Implementation:** This allows for further research and practical applications.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Incremental Improvement:** While the results are promising, the technique builds upon existing work (MCUNetV2, StreamNet). The novelty lies in the systematic approach rather than a fundamentally new concept.
    *   **Limited Experimental Scope:** The experimental results are preliminary. More extensive evaluations with a wider range of CNN architectures and applications would strengthen the claims.
    *   **Worst-Case Analysis:**  The worst-case analysis of the algorithm complexity is helpful but the practically observed complexity can vary.
    *   **Lack of Comparison:** It mentions that it can be used with kernel fusion, but a comparative benchmark could have added more impact.
    *   **No Accuracy Results:** The results and comparisons do not include the final accuracy of the networks, which is very important.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field by providing a more systematic and efficient way to optimize CNNs for MCUs. The open-source implementation will facilitate further research and adoption by practitioners. However, its impact will depend on the extent to which it can outperform existing techniques in a wider range of applications.

*   **Rigorous Rationale for Score:**  The paper presents a valuable contribution to the problem of deploying CNNs on resource-constrained MCUs. While it builds upon existing work, the systematic graph-based approach and pruning strategy offer a significant improvement over previous heuristic methods. The experimental results are promising, but more extensive evaluations are needed. The paper does a decent job of identifying the potential impact of the solution and is useful for researchers.
    It fails to compare with kernel fusion though, and doesn't mention any accuracy data with their RAM and performance tradeoffs.
